Relations between [China](5405.md) and [Japan](15573.md) entered a state of crisis in November 2025, after Japanese prime minister [Sanae Takaichi](8125532.md) said in the [Japanese parliament](163847.md) that a Chinese attack on [Taiwan](25734.md) could potentially constitute an "existential crisis for Japan" under the [Legislation for Peace and Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislation_for_Peace_and_Security), allowing Japan to take military action in [collective self-defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_self-defence). Following these remarks, the Chinese consul general in Osaka, [Xue Jian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xue_Jian), made threatening comments against Takaichi on [X (formerly Twitter)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X_\(formerly_Twitter\)), triggering a diplomatic row between the countries.

Both sides protested the other's remarks. In response to questions from Japanese parliamentarians, Takaichi refused to withdraw her remarks, arguing that they were consistent with the Japanese government's existing position on the issue. Japan requested that China take "appropriate measures" against Xue. China refused, demanded Takaichi retract her statements, and accused Japan of "[neo-militarism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-militarism)", issued numerous retaliatory measures against Japan, including issuing a [travel advisory](29372620.md), restricting travel and cultural exchanges, and cutting off seafood imports from the country. In 2026, the dispute further escalated with China restricting the export of [dual-use items](1003982.md) and [rare earth materials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rare_earth_industry_in_China) to Japan.

## Background

Main articles: [Political status of Taiwan](66555.md) and [Legislation for Peace and Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislation_for_Peace_and_Security)

See also: [Taiwan under Japanese rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_under_Japanese_rule)



Japanese Prime Minister [Sanae Takaichi](8125532.md) with Chinese leader [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) during the [APEC South Korea 2025](78485796.md), 31 October 2025

From 1895 to 1945, the [island of Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Island_of_Taiwan) along with the [Penghu islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penghu_islands) was under [Japanese colonial rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_under_Japanese_rule) by the terms of the [Treaty of Shimonoseki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Shimonoseki) in the aftermath of the [First Sino-Japanese War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Sino-Japanese_War). Following the [surrender of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surrender_of_Japan) in the conclusion of World War II, Taiwan was [handed over](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retrocession_of_Taiwan) to the [Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_\(1912%E2%80%931949\)) (ROC) in 1945, but the ROC soon lost its [mainland territory](55079.md) due to its defeat in the [Chinese Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Civil_War) and [retreated its government to Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retreat_of_the_government_of_the_Republic_of_China_to_Taiwan) in 1949. Japan formally "renounced all right, title, and claim" to Taiwan in [1952](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_San_Francisco), without naming the specific receiver of Taiwan's sovereignty, which began a long-standing [geopolitical dispute](66555.md). Since 1947, under the auspices of the [Supreme Commander for the Allied Powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Commander_for_the_Allied_Powers), the [Japanese constitution](62699.md) has maintained a clause of [Article 9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_9_of_the_Japanese_Constitution), which prohibits the use of force to resolve international disputes. From the 1980s the Japanese government has taken the stance that protection of the [sea lane](2018420.md) between Japan and the [Bashi Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bashi_Channel) south of Taiwan is permissible as self-defence,[^cite_note-1][^cite_note-2][^cite_note-3] and that it would not constitute collective self-defence.[^cite_note-4] In 2015, [Shinzo Abe](2634434.md)'s government [passed legislation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislation_for_Peace_and_Security) that allowed Japan to engage in [collective self-defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_defence) in specific cases, such as if an ally like the [United States](3434750.md) came under attack.[^cite_note-5] In 2021, after his resignation, Abe said that a Chinese attack on [Taiwan](25734.md), or what he referred to as a "Taiwan emergency", would be an emergency for Japan and the [US–Japan alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S.%E2%80%93Japan_alliance).[^cite_note-6]

[Sanae Takaichi](8125532.md), who was elected as the [Prime Minister of Japan](24833.md) in October 2025, has been generally seen as [pro-Taiwanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-Taiwanese_sentiment) and hawkish on China. In April 2025, she visited Taiwan and met with President [Lai Ching-te](22609914.md). She has repeated Abe's statement that a "Taiwan emergency is a Japan emergency."[^cite_note-7] Her election as [Liberal Democratic Party](164231.md) (LDP) leader also led [Komeito](163859.md), which maintains friendly ties with China, to break its [coalition](80439435.md) with the LDP.[^cite_note-8] Chinese leader [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) made an unusual move by not sending a congratulatory telegram on the day Takaichi assumed the post of prime minister, but a Japan–China summit meeting between Xi and Takaichi took place on 31 October at the [APEC South Korea 2025](78485796.md). However, after Takaichi held talks with Taiwan's former Vice Premier [Lin Hsin-i](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lin_Hsin-i) on 1 November, China lodged a protest with Japan, and Japan counter-argued, leading to the deterioration of the relationship.[^cite_note-9]

## Dispute

During deliberations in the [National Diet](163847.md) on 7 November, Japanese prime minister Sanae Takaichi said that a Chinese attack on Taiwan could constitute "[an existential crisis for Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislation_for_Peace_and_Security#Existential_crisis_situation)" under the [Legislation for Peace and Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislation_for_Peace_and_Security) formulated in 2015, allowing it to take military action in self-defence.[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-Chen-11][^cite_note-13] Under the conditions of Legislation, a survival-threatening situation refers to an attack on a country with close ties to Japan that threatens Japan's survival and poses a clear danger to the lives of its people as a legal prerequisite for Japan exercising its right to [collective self-defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_self-defence), even if Japan itself is not directly attacked.[^cite_note-Asahi_251114-14]

> [Japanese](15606.md): それが戦艦を使って、そして武力の行使も伴うものであれば、これはどう考えても存立危機事態になり得るケースであると私は考えます。
>
> \[If it were to involve the use of warships and the exercise of force, then by any measure, it could constitute a situation threatening Japan’s survival.]

— Sanae Takaichi[^cite_note-Asahi_251114-14][^cite_note-bbc20251118-15]

Takaichi made this remark in response to a question from opposition parliamentarian [Katsuya Okada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katsuya_Okada) about a hypothetical naval [blockade](293054.md) of the [Bashi Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bashi_Channel) between the Philippines and Taiwan.[^cite_note-16] In response to the comments, the Chinese consul-general in Osaka, [Xue Jian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xue_Jian), wrote on [X](9988187.md) that "we have no choice but to cut off that dirty neck that has lunged at us without a moment's hesitation. Are you ready?",[^cite_note-18] although the post was later deleted after a protest by the Japanese government.[^cite_note-Chen-11]

| **薛剑XueJian** (@xuejianosaka) tweeted: |
| --- |

[Japanese](15606.md):&#x20;

> 勝手に突っ込んできたその汚い首は一瞬の躊躇もなく斬ってやるしかない。覚悟が出来ているのか。😡

---

Translation:&#x20;

> &#x20;We have no choice but to cut off that dirty neck that has lunged at us without a moment's hesitation. Are you ready?😡&#x20;

8 November 2025[^cite_note-Tweetxuejianosaka-19]&#x20;

This exchange of words led to a diplomatic row between Japan and China.[^cite_note-20][^cite_note-21] Chinese officials condemned Takaichi's remarks as they cast doubt on Japan's stance regarding the [one China principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_China_principle) and constitutes [interference in China's internal affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-interventionism),[^cite_note-22][^cite_note-nikkei20251120-23] while Japanese officials and the US ambassador to Japan criticized Xue's comment.[^cite_note-Chen-11][^cite_note-24] The Taiwanese government attempted to keep a low profile regarding the conflict, with the opposition parties displaying mixed attitudes.[^cite_note-25] During further discussion in Parliament on 12 November, Takaichi said that her remarks were in accord with the government's longstanding position and would not be withdrawn. She also said that she would refrain from making statements about specific cases in the future.[^cite_note-26]

### Follow-up exchanges

&#x20;On 13 November, Chinese deputy foreign minister [Sun Weidong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun_Weidong) summoned the Japanese ambassador to China, [Kenji Kanasugi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenji_Kanasugi), to protest Sanae Takaichi's remarks. Kanasugi rejected the Chinese argument, explained the Japanese position, and protested Consul General Xue Jian's remarks.[^cite_note-27] On 14 November, Japanese Vice-Minister for Foreign Affairs Takehiro Funakoshi summoned the Chinese ambassador to Japan, [Wu Jianghao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wu_Jianghao), to protest Xue Jian's remarks. According to press releases by the Japanese and Chinese Ministries of Foreign Affairs, Wu said Takaichi's remarks were "complete misjudgment of the situation and an overestimation of Japan's capabilities" while "Funakoshi countered these statements based on the Japanese government's consistent and long-standing position."[^cite_note-28]



Permanent Representative of China to the United Nations [Fu Cong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fu_Cong)'s letter to United Nations Secretary General [António Guterres](471806.md) regarding China's position on Takaichi's remarks (2025)

On 17 November, [Masaaki Kanai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masaaki_Kanai), Director-general of the Asian and Oceanian Affairs Bureau of Japan's [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](868429.md), arrived in China and met with [Liu Jinsong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liu_Jinsong), Director-general of the [Department of Asian Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Asian_Affairs) of the Chinese Ministry of Foreign Affairs on 18 November.[^cite_note-29] During the meeting, Liu protested Takaichi's remarks and requested that they be retracted. Kanai responded by saying Japan's security policy did not change and protested Consul General Xue Jian's remarks and criticized China's moves to limit the movement of people between the two countries.[^cite_note-NHK-30][^cite_note-31] After the meeting, the Chinese side took the unusual step of allowing reporters into the Ministry of Foreign Affairs building to take pictures. Chinese media published pictures of Liu, who was wearing a [Chinese tunic suit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mao_suit) in the style of the [May Fourth Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_Fourth_Movement) and had the [national emblem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_emblem_of_China) pinned to his chest, speaking, while Kanai listened with his head down,[^cite_note-32] a move considered by [Nippon Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nippon_Television) to be China trying to manipulate public opinion.[^cite_note-33] Japanese Chief Cabinet Secretary [Minoru Kihara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minoru_Kihara) said that the Chinese side had unilaterally permitted photography without agreement from Japan.[^cite_note-34]

On 18 November, at the plenary session of the [United Nations General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eightieth_session_of_the_United_Nations_General_Assembly) on [Security Council](31956.md) reform, [Fu Cong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fu_Cong), [China's Permanent Representative to the United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_Representative_of_China_to_the_United_Nations), said Japan was "totally unqualified to demand a [permanent seat on the Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_members_of_the_United_Nations_Security_Council)".[^cite_note-35] A Japanese representative described Fu's accusations as groundless and said Tokyo had contributed to maintaining peace and prosperity in the world.[^cite_note-36] On 22 November, Fu delivered a letter to the UN Secretary-General [António Guterres](471806.md) to be distributed to all [UN member states](31969.md) which said if Japan were to intervene militarily in the event of a war between China and Taiwan, it would constitute an act of aggression and that China would exercise its right of self-defense under the [UN Charter](88805.md) and international law.[^cite_note-37] In an emailed statement to [Reuters](18998750.md), Japan's Foreign Ministry said it was aware of Fu's letter for the claim of military intervention which is "entirely unacceptable" and reiterated the country's commitment to peace.[^cite_note-38]

On 24 November, Japanese Ambassador to the UN [Kazuyuki Yamazaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazuyuki_Yamazaki?action=edit\&redlink=1) wrote a letter to Secretary-General Guterres, stating China's assertions were "erroneous" and that Japan's defense policy is exclusively defense-oriented, while also criticizing China's military build-up.[^cite_note-39] He also stated Japan's position on Taiwan had not changed.[^cite_note-Kyodo_News-40] On 1 December, Fu sent a second letter to Secretary-General Guterres as a reply to Yamazaki's letter, in which he wrote that Takaichi's statement was the "greatest challenge" which damaged relations between the two countries, and said Japan should "clearly reaffirm the one-China principle, immediately retract the erroneous remarks and take practical steps to honor its commitments to China".[^cite_note-41] He also called on the international community to "remain highly vigilant against Japan's ambitions to expand its military capabilities and revive militarism". In response, Chief Cabinet Secretary Kihara called China's allegations "not true at all" and "totally unacceptable", saying Japan has "consistently contributed to the peace and prosperity" since the end of World War II.[^cite_note-Kyodo_News-40] On 4 December, Japanese ambassador Yamazaki again issued a rebuttal, calling China's assertions "inconsistent with the facts, unsubstantiated, and are categorically unacceptable".[^cite_note-42]

On 15 December, at an open debate of the UN Security Council regarding leadership for peace, Fu urged Takaichi to withdraw her remarks, saying they had "gone against the trend", and said Japan had launched aggression 80 years ago "in the name of self-defense, bringing profound catastrophes" to China and Asia, saying that the world "must never allow the resurgence of militarism and fascism". Yamazaki replied by saying Japan had consistently pursued the path of a peace-loving nation since the end of World War II and that China's comments were not appropriate for the Security Council's agenda.[^cite_note-43] On 26 January 2026, Fu addressed a UN Security Council meeting on international rule of law by stating Takaichi's remarks constitute a "brazen intervention in China's internal affairs" and are an open breach of Japan's obligations as a country defeated in World War II, to which Japanese representative Kazuyuki responded by saying it was "regrettable" that the Chinese delegation made groundless statements against Japan.[^cite_note-44] On 18 February, Fu said that Japan was "attempting to intervene militarily in the Taiwan issue under the guise of collective self-defence", which he said "would constitute an aggression against China".[^cite_note-45]

On 18 May 2026, members of the [Japan–China Friendship Parliamentarians' Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan%E2%80%93China_Friendship_Parliamentarians'_Union), including Chairman Yu Moriyama, dined with Ambassador Wu Jianghao at the Chinese Embassy in Japan.[^cite_note-46] On 22 June, after attending the China International Supply Chain Promotion Expo, [Gaku Hashimoto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaku_Hashimoto), acting chairman of the Japan International Trade Promotion Association and member of the House of Representatives, held a meeting with [Hua Chunying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hua_Chunying), vice minister of the Chinese Ministry of Foreign Affairs, for about an hour. He was the first member of the Japanese National Diet to meet with a high-ranking Chinese government official since the beginning of the diplomatic crisis. The next day, when meeting with reporters, Gaku Hashimoto said, "We have found a way to communicate, which is a great achievement."[^cite_note-\:13-47][^cite_note-\:22-48] [Guo Jiakun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guo_Jiakun), spokesperson for the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, said that during the meeting, Hua Chunying expressed her condolences for the death of [Yōhei Kōno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Y%C5%8Dhei_K%C5%8Dno) and stated her position on issues such as Sino–Japanese relations.[^cite_note-49] She hoped that the Japanese business community would make positive efforts to promote the Japanese government to improve Sino–Japanese relations. Hua Chunying also welcomed the Japanese trade promotion delegation to visit China again.[^cite_note-\:132-50] In the same month, Kubota Masakazu, vice chairman of the [Japan Business Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_Business_Federation), exchanged views with local business groups when attending the Summer Davos Forum in [Dalian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalian) and said that "the conditions for restarting the delegation's visit to China have been met."[^cite_note-51]

On 22 July, Chinese Foreign Minister Wang Yi and Japanese Foreign Minister Toshimitsu Motegi attended a series of [ASEAN](106539.md) meetings in the Philippines. Wang Yi was absent from the dinner, the [ASEAN Plus Three](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ASEAN_Plus_Three) (China, Japan and South Korea) Foreign Ministers’ Meeting, the [East Asia Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Asia_Summit) and the ASEAN Regional Forum Foreign Ministers’ Meeting attended by Motegi.[^cite_note-52][^cite_note-\:16-53] On 23 July, Motegi told reporters that he had a brief conversation with Wang Yi, who had just finished the ASEAN Plus Three Foreign Ministers’ Meeting, when he entered the venue before the start of the ASEAN Plus Three Foreign Ministers’ Meeting. They talked about Sino–Japanese relations without the help of interpreters.[^cite_note-54][^cite_note-55] Lin Jian, a spokesperson for the Chinese Ministry of Foreign Affairs, said at a press conference on the same day that “Foreign Minister Wang Yi has no arrangements to meet with the Japanese side.”[^cite_note-\:16-53] On 25 July, [China Global Television Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Global_Television_Network) said that Wang Yi did not talk with the Japanese side.[^cite_note-56] On 27 July, Lin Jian said that “there was no brief exchange on site.”[^cite_note-57]

From 24 to 27 August, a cross-party delegation of Japanese parliamentarians, consisting of [Shinichi Isa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shinichi_Isa), a member of the House of Representatives from the [Centrist Reform Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centrist_Reform_Alliance), Gaku Hashimoto, a member from the [Liberal Democratic Party](164231.md), and [Yudai Kawamura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yudai_Kawamura), a member of the House of Representatives from [Komeito](163859.md), visited Beijing as the first such delegation since the diplomatic incident.[^cite_note-q505-58][^cite_note-59][^cite_note-60] The delegation visited robots, autonomous driving, and the [Xiong'an New Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xiong'an), and exchanged views with scholars from the Institute of Japanese Studies of the [Chinese Academy of Social Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Academy_of_Social_Sciences).[^cite_note-61][^cite_note-62][^cite_note-63] Both sides mentioned that the trip was initiated by several Japanese parliamentarians who wanted to visit China to seek dialogue.[^cite_note-64] [Hiroyuki Konishi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiroyuki_Konishi_\(politician,_born_1972\)), a member of the House of Councillors from the [Chiba at-large district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiba_at-large_district) and a member of the [Constitutional Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Democratic_Party_of_Japan) [of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Democratic_Party_of_Japan), who was originally scheduled to attend, canceled his plans due to the Chiba rainstorm.[^cite_note-\:21-65] Gaku Hashimoto returned to Japan midway through the visit.[^cite_note-\:21-65] The delegation met [Lu Kang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lu_Kang_\(diplomat\)), Deputy Head of the [International Department of the Chinese Communist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Department_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party).[^cite_note-66] The meeting on 27 August lasted for about two hours. Lu Kang said the "relations are in serious difficulty, and the root cause is clear" and said he hoped Japanese politicians could urge the nation's leadership to "handle the Taiwan issue honestly and earnestly according to the One-China principle".[^cite_note-67] He added that as long as Japanese Prime Minister Sanae Takaichi's parliamentary response to Taiwan in November of last year and her understanding of Taiwan remain unchanged, China will not change its policy toward Japan and will demand that Japan withdraw its response to Taiwan. Isa proposed to restart youth and cultural exchanges between Japan and China.[^cite_note-\:21-65][^cite_note-68]

From 13 to 15 September, eight young members of the Japan–China Friendship Parliamentarians' Union visited China, led by LDP member and former Vice Minister of Justice, [Masahiro Kōmura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masahiro_K%C5%8Dmura). On 14 September, the delegation met with Assistant Minister of Foreign Affairs Liu Bin and [Yang Wanming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yang_Wanming_\(diplomat\)?action=edit\&redlink=1), President of the [Chinese People's Association for Friendship with Foreign Countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_People's_Association_for_Friendship_with_Foreign_Countries), in Beijing. Komura said that being able to meet directly with officials of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs was an important step forward. During the talks, he raised issues such as rare earth minerals that the Japanese people were concerned about, and hoped that the contact between the two sides could develop into an intergovernmental dialogue.[^cite_note-69][^cite_note-70][^cite_note-71] The Chinese side urged the Japanese side to correct its mistakes and abide by the principles and spirit of the four political documents between China and Japan.[^cite_note-72]

From 27 to 30 September, a roughly 50-member delegation of Japan's Association for the Promotion of International Trade led by former foreign minister [Takeshi Iwaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takeshi_Iwaya) visited China.[^cite_note-73] On 28 September, Iwaya met with Chinese foreign minister Wang Yi, who said "Current Sino-Japanese relations are once again facing a severe situation, requiring normalization once more" and noted that 2027 marks the 55th anniversary of diplomatic ties between the two nations.[^cite_note-\:5-74] He said that "A country that challenges its post-war ‘peace constitution’ or attempts to rewrite the history of wartime aggression … cannot face the world, let alone open up a future" and urged Japan to "correct the erroneous words and deeds" of its leadership regarding Taiwan. He also called on Japan to "learn from historical lessons" on the 80th anniversary of the [Tokyo Trials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Military_Tribunal_for_the_Far_East). Iwaya said that the state of the current relations was "worrying", and that his organization would honor the legacy of Yōhei Kōno and work to return the relations back to the principles of the four foundational bilateral documents[^cite_note-\:6-75]. Iwaya also met with Chinese vice premier [He Lifeng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/He_Lifeng) on 29 September, who hoped Iwaya's assocition would "adhere to the correct historical direction and carry forward the tradition of friendship with China" and praised Japan as an economic and trade partner, welcoming Japanese companies.[^cite_note-\:5-74]

#### APEC China 2026

The [APEC 2026 Leaders Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/APEC_China_2026) will be hosted by China from 18 to 19 November 2026, set to be located in [Shenzhen](83368.md), China. As the host nation, Chinese leader Xi Jinping is expected to preside over the summit with attendance by leadership of APEC member states. *[Nikkei Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikkei_Asia)* speculated that Xi Jinping may extend an invitation to Sanae Takaichi in order secure CCP legitimacy ahead of party congress and highlight Xi Jinping as a "eminent global leader".[^cite_note-Nikkei2026Shenzhen-76] *The Yomiuri Shimbun* reported that Japanese government has tried to set up a face-to-face summit between Xi and Sanae Takaichi at the November APEC 2026 summit in China.[^cite_note-Yomiuri2026Takaichi-77] *Yomiuri* has reported that Chinese authorities have consistently declined contact with officials from the Japanese government, while personnel at the Japanese Embassy in China have, in most cases, been unable to secure meetings with Chinese counterparts.[^cite_note-Yomiuri2026Takaichi-77] The Japanese ambassador to China [Kenji Kanasugi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenji_Kanasugi) vowed "best effort" to arrange a leaders' summit at APEC between Xi Jinping and Sanae Takaichi, adding a meeting between the two leaders is "very important" as a breakthrough for thawing ties.[^cite_note-Kyodo2026Kanasugi-78]

On 15 May 2026, [Hitoshi Kawada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hitoshi_Kikawada), Minister of State for Special Missions (Co-operative Representative for Women and Men) of the Cabinet Office of Japan, attended the APEC Ministerial Meeting on Women and Economy held in Shanghai. He was the first Japanese cabinet minister to visit China after the diplomatic incident, but he did not meet with Chinese officials.[^cite_note-79] In the first ever ministerial meeting between Japan and China since the diplomatic crisis started, Japanese top trade minister [Akazawa Ryosei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryosei_Akazawa) reached out to briefly chat with Chinese counterpart Wang Wentao on the sidelines of the 2026 APEC trade ministers meeting in Suzhou, China,[^cite_note-Nikkei2026APEC-80] However, these talks were not considered formal talks, only a short conversation with no substantive discussions.[^cite_note-BusinessToday2026APEC-81] At the same time, Japanese Vice Minister of Foreign Affairs Iwao Horii also revealed that he had contact with Wang Wentao on the evening of 22 May.[^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83] As the Japanese government continues to attempt to arrange a meeting between the two leaders at APEC summit, China remains unresponsive.[^cite_note-Chosun2026-84] China has maintained its stance of refusing dialogue unless Prime Minister Takaichi retracts her Diet remarks.[^cite_note-Chosun2026-84] At the food security ministers’ meeting in August and the energy ministers’ meeting in September, Japanese officials also said they had brief conversations with Chinese officials.[^cite_note-85][^cite_note-86]

## Chinese retaliatory measures

### Military

See also: [Fourth Taiwan Strait Crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Taiwan_Strait_Crisis) and [Justice Mission-2025](81974787.md)

On 15 November, the [China Maritime Safety Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Maritime_Safety_Administration) announced that the [People's Liberation Army](66890.md) would conduct live-fire exercises in the central [Yellow Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_Sea) from 17 to 19 November, and that navigation in this area would be prohibited during this period.[^cite_note-87] The notice drew criticism from Taiwan, which accused Beijing of saber-rattling at Japan for political gain.[^cite_note-Su-88] On 16 November, the [China Coast Guard](15845458.md) announced that a formation of its ships carried out a patrol within the territorial waters of the [Senkaku Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senkaku_Islands), a [territory disputed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senkaku_Islands_dispute) between China Japan, and Taiwan.[^cite_note-89][^cite_note-90] The [Japan Coast Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_Coast_Guard) said it drove the ships away.[^cite_note-91] On 2 December, Chinese and Japanese coastguard vessels engaged in a standoff over the islands. China said it had implemented "necessary control measures" and driven a Japanese fishing boat away from the islands, while Japan stated it had intercepted and driven away two Chinese coastguard vessels that approached the fishing boat.[^cite_note-92]

From 6 to 7 December, the aircraft carrier [*Liaoning*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_aircraft_carrier_Liaoning) transited through the [Miyako Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miyako_Strait) between the islands of Okinawa and Miyakojima and began takeoff and landing drills with [Shenyang J-15](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenyang_J-15) jets; aircraft took off from and landed on the carrier roughly 100 times over two days.[^cite_note-Kobara-93] On 7 December, Japanese defense minister [Shinjirō Koizumi](27407451.md) accused China of two incidents on 6 December in which J-15 jets from the *Liaoning* carrier at locking their [fire-control radar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire-control_radar) at Japanese [F-15](11715.md) jets near the Miyako Strait. The Japanese government formally protested to the Chinese side. Takaichi also called the incident "extremely disappointing".[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-Kelly-95] Japanese vice foreign minister Takehiro Funakoshi summoned Chinese ambassador Wu Jianghao over the incident; for his part, Wu lodged "stern representation and strong protest" to Japan at the meeting.[^cite_note-96] The [PLA Navy](24918.md) spokesperson Wang Xuemeng responded by accusing Japan of a "slander and smear campaign" and said the *Liaoning* was carrying "routine carrier-based fighter jet flight training".[^cite_note-97] He also said [Japan Self-Defense Forces](2785204.md) aircraft had repeatedly approached and disrupted its fighter jet training.[^cite_note-98] Japanese officials later said that their Chinese counterparts didn't answer the hotline during the incident.[^cite_note-Kobara-93] Koizumi also said that while notified, Japan "did not receive sufficient information" regarding the drills, while Kihara said Japanese jets were far away from the Chinese jets while training.[^cite_note-99] The U.S. criticized the radar targeting of Japanese aircraft and reinforced the U.S. alliance with Japan.[^cite_note-Psaledakis-100] A [US State Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_State_Department) spokesperson also stated that "China’s actions are not conducive to regional peace and stability".[^cite_note-Psaledakis-100]

The aircraft carrier group travelled northeast from their position east of [Kikaijima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kikaijima) following the incident. A Chinese naval [Type 054 frigate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Type_054_frigate) also sailed through the Miyako Strait on 8 December, while another travelled through the Osumi Strait.[^cite_note-101] On 9 December, two Russian [Tupolev Tu-95](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tupolev_Tu-95) bombers, two Chinese [Xi'an H-6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xi'an_H-6) bombers and four Chinese [Shenyang J-16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenyang_J-16) fighter jets flew through the Miyako Strait into the Pacific Ocean as part of joint exercises.[^cite_note-102] On 10 December, two US [B-52](18933037.md) bombers flew together with three Japanese [F-35](11812.md) jets and three F-15 jets, which Japanese defense ministry said "reaffirmed their strong resolve to prevent any unilateral attempt to change the status quo by force".[^cite_note-103] On 29 December, the PLA's [Eastern Theater Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Theater_Command) announced a major military exercise around Taiwan codenamed [Justice Mission 2025](81974787.md), involving the command's [land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Liberation_Army_Ground_Force), [sea](24918.md), [air](381445.md), and [rocket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Liberation_Army_Rocket_Force) forces.[^cite_note-104] On 27 January 2026, Reuters reported that since late 2025, the Japanese government had been advising fishers to avoid the Senkaku Islands in order to not escalate relations with China.[^cite_note-105] On 13 February, the Japanese government announced it had seized a Chinese fishing boat in [Japan's exclusive economic zone](58123407.md) near [Nagasaki Prefecture](221380.md).[^cite_note-106] Japan subsequently released the captain of the ship after China submitted documents guaranteeing payment of bail.[^cite_note-107]

### Cultural exchange

Further information (in Chinese): [Restriction of Japanese culture by China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Restriction_of_Japanese_culture_by_China?action=edit\&redlink=1)

On 16 November, the Japanese [think tank](37101.md) [Genron NPO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genron_NPO) stated the 20th [Beijing–Tokyo Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Tokyo_Summit) and the release of the results of a joint Sino-Japanese opinion poll would be postponed after it received a request for cancellation from its Chinese partner, the [China International Communications Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_International_Communications_Group), in which it blamed Takaichi's remarks for undermining the atmosphere for normal exchanges between the two countries.[^cite_note-108][^cite_note-Sing_Tao_Daily-109] A delegation from [Xuzhou, Jiangsu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xuzhou) cancelled its visit to its sister city, [Handa, Aichi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Handa,_Aichi).[^cite_note-110] On 17 November, the release of Japanese films *[Crayon Shin-chan the Movie: Super Hot! The Spicy Kasukabe Dancers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crayon_Shin-chan_the_Movie:_Super_Hot!_The_Spicy_Kasukabe_Dancers)* and *[Cells at Work!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cells_at_Work!)*, which were originally scheduled to be released in China, were temporarily postponed.[^cite_note-111] The decision was made by the [China Film Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Film_Administration), which it said was based on a reassessment of the overall market outlook of Japanese film imports and sentiment of Chinese audiences, and that it had paused the approval process for new movies from Japan.[^cite_note-112] Hong Kong's public broadcaster [RTHK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTHK) also stopped airings of *Cells at Work!*[^cite_note-113] On the same evening, [QQ Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QQ_Music) announced the cancellation of a [JO1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JO1) fan meeting that was scheduled to be held in [Guangzhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guangzhou).[^cite_note-114] At the end of November 2025, a "latest notice" from Shanghai circulated on Weibo, which allegedly prohibited underground idols from singing Japanese songs and required them to sing Chinese songs. Dance performances could only play Chinese songs, and the policy was fully implemented in early December. In July 2026, relevant departments in several districts of Shanghai verbally instructed that songs with Japanese lyrics should not be sung on stage, and some underground idols had to replace Japanese songs with Chinese songs.[^cite_note-\:8-115]

On 18 November, the 8th Japan–China Friendship Exchange Conference in the Western Japan Region event originally scheduled to be held by the Consulate General of China in Osaka in [Hiroshima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiroshima) on 21 November was cancelled at its request due to security concern. Xue Jian was originally scheduled to attend the conference.[^cite_note-Sing_Tao_Daily-109] On 19 November, a concert by [Kokia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kokia_\(singer\)) was cancelled after her band was denied access to their venue.[^cite_note-Chen-2-116] On 25 November, Hong Kong singer [Ekin Cheng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ekin_Cheng) cancelled a planned performance in Tokyo in December.[^cite_note-117] On the same day, *[The Asahi Shimbun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Asahi_Shimbun)* reported that [censors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_censorship_in_China) appeared to be removing comments and videos on Chinese social media platforms which expressed views supportive of Japan or that deviated from the government's official line on Takaichi's remarks.[^cite_note-118] On 28 November, Japanese singer [Ayumi Hamasaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayumi_Hamasaki) announced the cancellation of a concert in [Shanghai](27643.md) scheduled for 29 November as part of the [I Am Ayu Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Am_Ayu_Tour);[^cite_note-119] she later performed in front of an empty stage.[^cite_note-120] During an appearance in Shanghai, Japanese singer Maki Otsuki's performance was interrupted mid-song, when the lights and sound were cut, and was asked to leave the stage, followed by the cancellation of a Japanese [anime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anime) event.[^cite_note-121] [Japanese idol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_idol) girl group [Momoiro Clover Z](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Momoiro_Clover_Z) also cancelled a planned performance.[^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123] In the same day, *[South China Morning Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_China_Morning_Post)* reported that China had cancelled youth exchange programs with Japan.[^cite_note-124] In December, [CNN](62028.md) estimated that performances and fan meet-and-greets from at least 30 Japanese performers had been canceled.[^cite_note-125] In reporting on the cancellations of events in China featuring Japanese musicians, Reuters noted that the Chinese government had a history of using cultural boycotts against countries as a form of economic coercion during diplomatic disputes.[^cite_note-Chen-2-116]

On 4 December, a [Hatsune Miku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hatsune_Miku) themed exhibition, which was scheduled to be held in Shanghai from 18 December 2025 to 1 March 2026, was postponed indefinitely.[^cite_note-Minoru-126] K-pop groups have also been affected by the cultural restrictions, with concerts and fan events featuring groups with Japanese members being cancelled. The activities of the South Korean entertainment company-owned girl group [Say My Name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Say_My_Name_\(group\)),[^cite_note-127] and boy group [Hi-Fi Un!corn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hi-Fi_Un!corn) in China were cancelled.[^cite_note-\:32-128] On 6 December, South Korean boy band [Close Your Eyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Close_Your_Eyes_\(group\)) held a fan meeting in [Hangzhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hangzhou) without Japanese member Kenshin, while a fan meeting event featuring trainees from Incode Entertainment who starred in [Mnet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mnet_\(TV_channel\))’s [Boys II Planet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boys_II_Planet) was abruptly canceled; the lineup included two Japanese members. The [Le Sserafim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Sserafim)'s meet-and-greet event for its single "[Spaghetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spaghetti_\(song\))" scheduled for 14 December in Shanghai was cancelled; the group has two Japanese members.[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-\:25-130] On 6 December, Hong Kong's [Leisure and Cultural Services Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leisure_and_Cultural_Services_Department) cancelled the screening of three Japanese films – *[Kamome Shokudo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamome_Shokudo)*, *[Tampopo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tampopo)* and *[Sweet Bean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweet_Bean)* – at the Food for Thought – A Cinematic Feast running from 6 December to 18 January.[^cite_note-131] On 7 December, Chinese video game company [miHoYo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MiHoYo) announced a two-week delay for a major update to their game *[Honkai: Star Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honkai:_Star_Rail)*, in which a new region widely teased to have Japanese influence was supposed to be released.[^cite_note-132] MiHoYo has apologized to players regarding the delay and issued in-game compensation.[^cite_note-133] On 9 December, [Studio Ghibli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Studio_Ghibli) announced that an exhibition in [Guangzhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guangzhou) based on the works of Japanese animator [Hayao Miyazaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hayao_Miyazaki) had been cancelled.[^cite_note-134]

On 10 December, a [Pokémon Trading Card Game](431322.md) tournament scheduled for January 2026 in Shanghai was postponed.[^cite_note-Minoru-126] In December, it was announced that the two pandas at the [Ueno Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ueno_Zoo), Xiao Xiao and Lei Lei, would return to China in January 2026, ahead of schedule;[^cite_note-Murakami-135] they returned to China on 28 January 2026.[^cite_note-136] In the same month, it was also announced that the Comicup comic convention in Hangzhou that would take place from 27 to 28 December would ban Japanese-themed content and derivative works including Japanese anime and manga.[^cite_note-137] On 11 December, Japanese singer [Lisa Ono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lisa_Ono)'s tour to Hangzhou, Guangzhou and Shenzhen from 19 to 244 December was cancelled.[^cite_note-138] On 18 December 2025, Japanese band [Roselia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roselia_\(band\))'s concert scheduled for 16 to 17 January 2026 was cancelled.[^cite_note-\:12-139] On 27 December 2025, Japanese rock group [Zutomayo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zutomayo)'s Shanghai tour scheduled for 28 March 2026 was cancelled.[^cite_note-140] On 30 December 2025, Japanese singer [Mika Nakashima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mika_Nakashima)'s Macau concert scheduled for 14 March 2026 was cancelled.[^cite_note-141] On 13 January, Japanese singer Nana Mizuki's Shanghai concert scheduled for 7 to 8 March was cancelled.[^cite_note-142] On 19 January, Japanese media personality [Shoko Nakagawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shoko_Nakagawa)'s 18 March Shanghai event was suddenly cancelled.[^cite_note-143] The South Korean [MBC TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MBC_TV) station's event [Show! Music Core](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Show!_Music_Core) in Macau, scheduled for 7 to 8 February, was cancelled on 28 January because the group that appeared included Japanese members.[^cite_note-144] On 16 February, Japanese signer [Koda Kumi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koda_Kumi) had her 21 March concert at the [AsiaWorld–Expo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AsiaWorld%E2%80%93Expo) in Hong Kong cancelled.[^cite_note-145] On 27 February, Japanese [YouTube](3524766.md) group HIMEHINA's Shanghai concert scheduled for July was cancelled.[^cite_note-146] On 14 March, Japanese band [King Gnu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Gnu)'s Shanghai concert scheduled for 13 to 14 June was cancelled.[^cite_note-147] On 9 April, Japanese rock band [One Ok Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Ok_Rock) had their concert in Hong Kong scheduled for 2 to 3 May cancelled.[^cite_note-148] On 21 April, Japanese singer [Fujii Kaze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fujii_Kaze) announced his Hong Kong concert was cancelled.[^cite_note-149] On 26 August, Japanese musician [Vaundy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaundy) announced his Shanghai concert scheduled for 14 to 15 November was cancelled.[^cite_note-150]

On 19 March 2026, Japanese civic groups said they had not received an invitation to the cherry blossom friendship forest planting event in [Wuxi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wuxi), Jiangsu. This event had been held since 1988, and this was the first time that Japan has not been invited except in years when infectious diseases are prevalent.[^cite_note-151] On 9 April, Japanese civic groups notified relevant organizations that the 3rd Japan University China Tour Education Exhibition, originally scheduled to begin on 11 April, could not be held due to "force majeure." Sources familiar with the matter revealed that this was due to pressure from Chinese authorities on the event venue.[^cite_note-152] In May, according to multiple media reports, some key Chinese universities suspended their exchange programs with Japan and halted the dispatch of Chinese students to Japan, citing "international environmental impact". In addition, due to the suspension of student exchange programs by some Chinese partner schools, some Japanese students were unable to go to China for exchange.[^cite_note-153] The number of Japanese anime imported by the Chinese video website [Bilibili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilibili) in 2026 was drastically reduced compared to previous years, with only 1 new anime in January,[^cite_note-154] and 0 in April.[^cite_note-155] No Japanese films were screened at the [Shanghai International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanghai_International_Film_Festival) in June 2026.[^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157] The Shanghai Film Festival has never completely banned films from any country before.[^cite_note-158] The publication of related books in Japan has also been restricted, and the issuance of [ISBNs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN) has been blocked.[^cite_note-159][^cite_note-160] No Japanese artists were invited to the Bilibili World exhibition held in Shanghai in July 2026.[^cite_note-161] Lin Quanzhong, a specially appointed researcher in international politics at the Institute of Oriental Culture, [University of Tokyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Tokyo), told Kyodo News on 14 July that he received an invitation from the Center for Japanese Studies at [Shanghai Jiao Tong University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanghai_Jiao_Tong_University) in early May to attend a lecture on "Looking at Chinese History from the Perspective of Japanese History" to be held at the end of May. However, he received a notice of cancellation a few days before his departure and did not know the reason. He said that "Affected by the deterioration of Sino-Japanese relations, academic activities related to Japan in China have been almost frozen."[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163]

On 14 November, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and the [Ministry of Culture and Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Culture_and_Tourism_\(China\)) advised Chinese citizens to avoid traveling to Japan;[^cite_note-\:1-164][^cite_note-\:2-165] Prior to the dispute, China was the largest source of tourists to Japan.[^cite_note-Northrop-175] [Air China](297958.md) (including its subsidiary [Air Macau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_Macau)), [China Southern Airlines](382884.md), [China Eastern Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Eastern_Airlines), [Hainan Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hainan_Airlines), [Shandong Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shandong_Airlines), [Sichuan Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sichuan_Airlines), and [XiamenAir](1287532.md) subsequently announced that tickets for flights to Japan with travel dates before 31 December could be refunded or changed free of charge.[^cite_note-176][^cite_note-177][^cite_note-tdm-167] On 5 December, they extended this policy for another three months to 28 March.[^cite_note-178] On 26 January 2026, this policy was further extended by seven months, applying to flights to and from Japan as well as transfers scheduled between 29 March and 24 October.[^cite_note-179]

News reports in November 2025 stated that [Nanjing Lukou International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanjing_Lukou_International_Airport) required all passengers going to Japan to use the manual channel and be questioned by customs officers.[^cite_note-180] On 16 November, both [Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_Bureau_\(Hong_Kong\)) and Macau updated their travel safety guidelines, reminding those who planned to go to or were already in Japan to be vigilant.[^cite_note-\:3-166][^cite_note-tdm-167] On the same day, the [Ministry of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_\(China\)) warned Chinese citizens to be cautious about going to Japan to study;[^cite_note-181] China is the largest source of foreign students in Japan.[^cite_note-Northrop-175] On 18 November, *[Financial Times](136566.md)* reported that several [state-owned enterprises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State-owned_enterprises_of_China) restricted their employees from travelling to Japan.[^cite_note-182] In reaction to the travel warning, tourism agencies in China received a large number of cancellation requests for group tours to Japan, while others announced the suspension of sales of related tourism products.[^cite_note-183] Some hotels and attractions in Japan received notices of cancellation of reservations by Chinese tourists.[^cite_note-NHK-30] On 19 November, the Chinese cruise ship *[Adora Mediterranea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adora_Mediterranea)* cancelled its planned stop at Hirara Port on [Miyako Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miyako_Island) on 20 November.[^cite_note-184] On 8 December, the Jian Zhen Hao ferry from Shanghai to [Osaka](52230.md) and [Kobe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kobe) was cancelled following a request from the Chinese.[^cite_note-185] Additionally, several universities such as the [Fudan University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fudan_University) suspended exchange programs with Japan.[^cite_note-186]

The [Nikkei 225](40293.md) of the [Tokyo Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tokyo_Stock_Exchange) fell on the morning of 17 November, with [Fast Retailing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fast_Retailing), which is related to the Chinese market, and Mitsukoshi Isetan, which is related to overseas tourist spending, experiencing significant declines.[^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196] Travel statistics cited by *[The Washington Post](102226.md)*, *[Sankei Shimbun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sankei_Shimbun)* and [Bloomberg News](3057255.md) estimated that about 543,000 airline tickets from China to Japan – representing around 40% of total Chinese airline bookings and 20% of China-Japan flight routes – were cancelled by 20 November and that around 30% of the 1.44 million planned trips from China to Japan through the end of December had been cancelled.[^cite_note-Northrop-175][^cite_note-ss111725-197][^cite_note-bn112125-198] The outlets also cited economic analyses which estimated a loss of \$500 million–\$1.2 billion to the Japanese economy as a result of the cancellations; if the travel boycotts persisted, they said those loses could rise from \$9 billion to over \$11 billion (¥1.79 trillion [yen](34392.md), equivalent to 0.29% of Japan's GDP).[^cite_note-Northrop-175][^cite_note-ss111725-197][^cite_note-bn112125-198] On 24 November, Yicai reported that Chinese airlines had cancelled 41 flights across the 12 routes.[^cite_note-199] On 25 November, Bloomberg News reported that Chinese government instructed airlines to reduce the number of flights to Japan through March 2026.[^cite_note-200] By 29 November, *[Nikkei Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikkei_Asia)* estimated Chinese airlines had canceled 904 flights for December, 16% of the initial total of 5,548, which was equivalent to around 156,000 seats.[^cite_note-201] On 1 December, the [China Central Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Central_Television) reported that more than 1,900 flights from mainland China to Japan for December, representing 40% of the total flights, had been cancelled.[^cite_note-202]

In reporting on the travel warning issued by the Chinese government, [NHK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NHK) pointed out that China often used economic pressure as a means of diplomatic friction, such as the boycott of Japanese goods in 2012 and the ban on Korean entertainment in 2017.[^cite_note-204] Decrease in Chinese tourism to Japan has also been marked by a corresponding increase of bookings by Chinese tourists to locations such as South Korea and Russia.[^cite_note-205][^cite_note-206] In December, the [Japan National Tourism Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_National_Tourism_Organization) revealed that arrivals from China in November had increased just 3% from the same period in 2024, compared to an increase of 40.7% in the first ten months of 2025. The figures also showed that the number of arrivals had dropped from 716,700 in October to 562,600 in November. Bloomberg News calculated that this had led to an economic loss of \$367 million (¥57 billion).[^cite_note-207] On 22 December, Flight Manager analyzed that flights across 46 routes covering a total of 38 airports had been cancelled by Chinese airlines for the upcoming two weeks.[^cite_note-208] On 25 December, Nippon Television reported the Chinese government had instructed domestic travel agencies to reduce the number of people visiting Japan to 60% of levels to date.[^cite_note-Murakami-2-209]

On 20 January 2026, Japan's [Ministry of Land, Infrastructure, Transport and Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Land,_Infrastructure,_Transport_and_Tourism) announced that the number of tourists from China in December 2025 had fallen further to 330,000. down 45% from the same month a year earlier.[^cite_note-210] On 26 January, China's foreign ministry warned its citizens from visiting Japan during the Chinese New Year, citing "a surge in crimes targeting Chinese citizens" and earthquakes.[^cite_note-211] In January 2026, 385,300 people from China visited Japan, representing a decline of 60.7% from the same period in 2025.[^cite_note-212] In February 2026, 396,400 people from China visited Japan, marking a decline of 45.2% from last year.[^cite_note-213] In March 2026, 291,600 people from China visited Japan, which was a decline of 55.9% from last year.[^cite_note-214] In the same month, there were 2,711 flights between the two countries, down from 5,512 flights in March 2025.[^cite_note-215] On the other hand, Kyodo News reported in April that a manager of a major Japanese travel agency revealed that due to the reduction in Sino–Japanese flights, the number of Japanese tourists to China has decreased by 90%.[^cite_note-216]

On 17 November, the mayor of [Shimonoseki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimonoseki) cancelled his planned business trip to [Suzhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suzhou) after receiving a request for postponements from Chinese officials the day before.[^cite_note-218] On 19 November, [Kyodo News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyodo_News) and other media reported that China informed Japan through diplomatic channels that it would temporarily halt imports of Japanese seafood. Sources told Kyodo News that China stated it was necessary to monitor the testing procedures for the treated water at the [Fukushima Daiichi Nuclear Power Plant](11986472.md). However, China did not state how long it would take for the "improvements to be completed".[^cite_note-219] Spokesperson Mao Ning also confirmed the news, but did not mention that it was for the purpose of boycott. She only said that, to her knowledge, it was because China had not yet received technical data from Japan. She added that under the current circumstances, there was "no market" for Japanese seafood imports.[^cite_note-Taylor-220] On the same day, Kyodo News reported that intergovernmental consultations to resume Japanese beef exports to China were suspended.[^cite_note-Taylor-220]

Japanese guests and media were prohibited from the biannual Aero Asia show of the Zhuhai International Airshow Center starting on 27 November.[^cite_note-221] On 28 November, [Japan Business Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_Business_Federation) Chairman Tsutsui Yoshinobu met with Chinese ambassador Wu Jianghao, telling him Japanese businesses hoped to promote smooth exchanges with China and build good relations.[^cite_note-222] On 30 November, *[The Nikkei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Nikkei)* reported that Liu Jinsong visited a major Japanese manufacturer in [Dalian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalian) to assure the company to feel safe while conducting business.[^cite_note-223] On 7 December, *[Yomiuri Shimbun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yomiuri_Shimbun)* reported that Japanese companies had faced delays in securing the approval process for importing [rare earths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rare_earth_industry_in_China) from China.[^cite_note-224] On 31 December, the Japan–China Economic Association announced that a delegation of around 200 business leaders will skip a trip originally scheduled 20 January, first time the trip was skipped since 2012 and 2020, the latter due to the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md).[^cite_note-225]

On 6 January 2026, the [Ministry of Commerce](638506.md) announced that all [dual-use items](1003982.md) are banned from being exported to Japan for military use effective immediately.[^cite_note-226] In reaction, Japanese vice foreign minister Takehiro Funakoshi summoned Chinese ambassador Wu Jianghao to lodge a strong protest and demand the withdrawal of the [sanctions](78786857.md); Wu rejected the demand, calling the restrictions "completely justified, reasonable and lawful". Ministry of Commerce spokesperson He Yadong stated the restrictions were aimed at "Japanese military users, for military purposes, and for any other end-use applications that contribute to enhancing Japan's military capabilities" in order to "preventing remilitarization and attempts to acquire nuclear weapons", and said it would not affect nonmilitary trade.[^cite_note-227] On 7 January, the Ministry of Commerce launched a dumping probe into [dichlorosilane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dichlorosilane) imports from Japan.[^cite_note-228] On 8 January, *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* reported China had started restricting exports of [rare earth materials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rare_earth_industry_in_China) to Japan.[^cite_note-229] On the same day, Kyodo News reported that Chinese customs were delaying the clearance of Japanese sake and food items imports to China.[^cite_note-230] By June 2026, Chinese exports of [terbium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terbium) and [dysprosium oxide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dysprosium_oxide) to Japan since November 2025 was at zero and only a small shipment of [yttrium oxide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yttrium_oxide) was exported to Japan.[^cite_note-rareearth-june26-231] On 8 September 2026, the Ministry of Commerce announced that companies would be required to provide cash deposits of up to 99.2% when importing dichlorosilane from Japan.[^cite_note-232]

On 24 February 2026, the Ministry of Commerce announced that 20 companies were to be placed on the export control list, and 20 more companies separately on the export control watch list.[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234] On 29 June 2026, the Ministry of Commerce added an additional 20 Japanese companies on the export control list, and an additional 20 Japanese companies separately on the export control watch list.[^cite_note-june-exportban-235]
