[Oki Electric Industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oki_Electric_Industry) and four of its subsidiaries: OKI Com-Echoes, OKI Circuit Technology, OKI Nextech, OKI Engineering YDK Technologies Nihon Denji Sokki [Howa Machinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howa) [Hosoya Pyro-Engineering](https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E7%B4%B0%E8%B0%B7%E7%81%AB%E5%B7%A5) Fujikura Parachute, subsidiary of [Fujikura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fujikura) |

### Security

On 15 November, the [Ministry of State Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_State_Security_\(China\)) announced that it recently "cracked a series of espionage cases involving Japanese intelligence agencies infiltrating and stealing secrets from China" and criticized Takaichi for "persisting in her erroneous remarks without remorse despite repeated solemn representations from China".[^cite_note-240] According to Changanjie Zhishi, a social media account run by the *[Beijing Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Daily)*, the MSS has been actively taking action against the frequent cases of Japanese espionage in recent years.[^cite_note-241]

## Japanese measures

On 17 November, the [Japanese Embassy in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_Japan,_Beijing) reminded Japanese people in China to take safety precautions and avoid crowded places where Japanese people are gathered.[^cite_note-242] On 18 November, Japan's [Ministry of Education, Culture, Sports, Science and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education,_Culture,_Sports,_Science_and_Technology) issued a notice requiring Japanese schools in China to ensure the safety of children, students and international students.[^cite_note-243] Japan's chief cabinet secretary Kihara said the advisories were made in response to a surge in anti-Japanese sentiment in the Chinese media.[^cite_note-244]

## Reactions in China and Japan

### China

#### Government

On 10 November, Chinese [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](7453497.md) spokesperson [Lin Jian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lin_Jian) protested Takaichi's remarks, saying China had lodged a complaint with Japan over her remarks and that Japan needed to reflect on its historical crimes and stop interfering in China's internal affairs.[^cite_note-245] He also defended Xue's post by saying it was a personal one and that China would not tolerate any foreign interference when it came to Taiwan.[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-bbc20251118-15] Citing the [Mukden incident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mukden_incident) of 1931, the [Chinese embassy in Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_China,_Tokyo) noted that Japan had invaded its neighbours including China under the pretext of "existential crisis".[^cite_note-247] The embassy also stated on 21 November that China could invoke the [enemy state clauses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Enemy_State_Clause) in the [UN Charter](88805.md), implying possible military action against Japan.[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249] On 14 November, [Ministry of National Defense](33632648.md) spokesperson Jiang Bin said that if Japan intervened in a war between China and Taiwan, then it would suffer a crushing defeat by the People's Liberation Army.[^cite_note-250] Each of PLA's five [theater commands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theater_command) have also released statements condemning Takaichi.[^cite_note-251] On 16 November, foreign ministry spokesperson Mao Ning said Premier [Li Qiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li_Qiang) had no plans to meet with Takaichi during the upcoming [G20 summit in South Africa](78509872.md); Japan had reportedly tried to arrange a Takaichi-Li meeting on the sidelines of the summit to reduce tensions between the two countries.[^cite_note-nikkei20251120-23] While citing the 1972 [Japan–China Joint Communiqué](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan%E2%80%93China_Joint_Communiqu%C3%A9), Mao further called on Japan to abide by its commitment to China over Taiwan and for Takaichi to retract her comments.[^cite_note-252]

On 20 November, China's [Ministry of Commerce](638506.md) spokesperson He Yongqian said trade cooperation with Japan had been "severely damaged" and urged Takaichi to retract her comments.[^cite_note-253] On the same day, Mao announced the cancellation of a meeting between culture ministers that was scheduled for November with South Korea and Japan.[^cite_note-254] On 22 November, Chinese Foreign Minister [Wang Yi](18211536.md) said Takaichi's comments crossed a red line which demanded a resolute response from China and that all countries had a responsibility to prevent the resurgence of [Japanese militarism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_militarism).[^cite_note-255][^cite_note-256] He additionally said that China would never allow Japanese right-wing forces to turn back the tide of history, foreign forces to encroach on China's Taiwan area, and a revival of Japanese militarism, which was picked by Chinese state media as the "Three Never Allows".[^cite_note-Dotson-257] On 24 November, Hong Kong Chief Executive [John Lee Ka-chiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Lee_Ka-chiu) criticized Takaichi's remarks and said they had caused his government to cast doubt on the value of exchanges between China and Japan.[^cite_note-b241125-258] Hong Kong also halted exchanges with Japan's consulate and canceled several official events with the country.[^cite_note-b241125-258] In addition to statements made in person or on a government website, Chinese officials made posts on X targeting Japan and Takaichi.[^cite_note-259] The X accounts of the Chinese Ministry of Foreign Affairs and the Ministry of National Defense issued warnings in both English and Japanese to Japan.[^cite_note-260] On 24 November, Chinese leader Xi Jinping and President Trump held a phone call, where Xi said "China and the United States once fought side by side against fascism and militarism, and should now work together to safeguard the outcomes of World War II" and that the [retrocession of Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retrocession_of_Taiwan) was an "integral part of the post-World War II international order", while the Chinese readout stated Trump "understands how important the Taiwan question is to China."[^cite_note-261][^cite_note-262]

During a call with French presidential policy advisor [Emmanuel Bonne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmanuel_Bonne) on 27 November and a talk with United Kingdom security advisor [Jonathan Powell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Powell_\(civil_servant\)) on 28 November, Foreign Minister Wang Yi called on both countries to abide by the one-China principle and called on them to "safeguard the outcomes" of World War II. Wang also condemned Takaichi's statement during his talk with Bonne and elaborated China's position on Japan-related issues to Powell.[^cite_note-263] On 9 December, Wang told German foreign minister [Johann Wadephul](50782003.md) that Takaichi was "trying to exploit the Taiwan question – the very territory Japan colonized for half a century, committing countless crimes against the Chinese people – to provoke trouble and threaten China militarily", which he said was "completely unacceptable".[^cite_note-264] On 13 December, at the [Nanjing Massacre Memorial Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanjing_Massacre_Memorial_Day), [CCP Organization Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organization_Department_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party) head and Politburo member [Shi Taifeng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shi_Taifeng) gave a speech saying that under the leadership of the CCP, China had beaten invaders and become a great nation, continuing by saying that "History has proven and will continue to prove that any attempt to revive militarism, challenge the postwar international order, and undermine world peace and stability is doomed to failure".[^cite_note-265] On 14 December, China sent [National Health Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Health_Commission) International Cooperation Department director general Feng Yong, a working-level official rather than a minister, to a meeting of health ministers from Japan, China and South Korea in [Seoul](19159283.md); the three countries agreed to strengthen trilateral cooperation on universal health coverage and mental health through technology and artificial intelligence.[^cite_note-266] On 15 December, the Chinese foreign minister announced sanctions against former Japanese chief of staff of the joint staff [Shigeru Iwasaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shigeru_Iwasaki) for being a consultant to Taiwan's [Executive Yuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Yuan), saying he had "openly colluded with the ‘Taiwan independence’ separatist forces".[^cite_note-Wang-267]

From 12 to 16 December, foreign minister Wang Yi visited the United Arab Emirates, Saudi Arabia and Jordan. Wang later told Chinese media that explained China's position and made clear the country's firm opposition to Takaichi interfering in China's domestic affairs.[^cite_note-268] On 16 December, Chinese foreign ministry spokesperson [Guo Jiakun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guo_Jiakun) said that "On key issues, Japan is still 'squeezing toothpaste' and 'burying nails,' attempting to obfuscate and muddle through", and that China is "firmly opposed to this".[^cite_note-269] On 17 December, Nippon Television reported that the Chinese foreign ministry had summoned ambassadors from [ASEAN](106539.md) countries, including the Philippines and Singapore, to criticize Takaichi's statements and urge them to support China, adding that similar requests were also reportedly made to ambassadors from France, Germany and other countries when they met with the Chinese foreign ministry.[^cite_note-270] In response to the delegation of Japanese parliamentary members’ visit to Taiwan on 22 December, the Chinese foreign ministry lodged "solemn representations with the Japanese side",[^cite_note-Reuters-271] while foreign ministry spokesperson Lin Jian said that Japanese parliamentarians "paying a visit to China's Taiwan region" were acts that would "seriously violate" the political foundations of the bilateral relationship between China and Japan.[^cite_note-Zhuang-272] On 30 December, Wang Yi told the Symposium on the International Situation and China's Foreign Relations that Takaichi was "openly challenging" China's territorial integrity.[^cite_note-273]

On 1 January 2026, Wang Yi held a call with South Korean foreign minister [Cho Hyun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cho_Hyun_\(diplomat\)), telling him that "certain political forces in Japan" were trying to "reverse the course of history and whitewash" Japan's "crimes of aggression and colonialism".[^cite_note-274] on 8 January, China's Ministry of Foreign Affairs and Ministry of National Defense jointly issued a statement saying that "moves of the Japanese side form a grave challenge to the international nuclear non-proliferation regime based on the Treaty on the Non-Proliferation of Nuclear Weapons and the postwar international order", referring to Japan possibly seeking to revise the [Three Non-Nuclear Principles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Non-Nuclear_Principles), discussing the potential introduction of nuclear-powered submarines, and calling for boosting "extended deterrence".[^cite_note-Bloomberg_News-275] In January, [Chinese embassy in the US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_China,_Washington,_D.C.) spokesperson Liu Pengyu wrote an article in the *[Executive Intelligence Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Intelligence_Review)*, calling for the US and China to work together to prevent the resurgence of Japanese militarism, writing that "Preventing militarism from harming the world again is in the common interest of both our countries".[^cite_note-Sun-276] The Chinese foreign ministry further stated that Takaichi's remarks had "severely [hurt the feelings of the Chinese people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurt_the_feelings_of_the_Chinese_people)."[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278] *[The New York Times](30680.md)* reported in May that when Chinese leader [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) met with British prime minister [Keir Starmer](18681491.md), who was [visiting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_visit_by_Keir_Starmer_to_China) China in late January, he told Starmer that the tensions were entirely Japan's fault.[^cite_note-279] On 14 February, at the [Munich Security Conference](1464620.md), Foreign Minister Wang Yi said that Takaichi's remarks was "the first time in 80 years that a Japanese Prime Minister has uttered such words. It directly violates China’s territorial sovereignty, and it directly challenges the fact that Taiwan has been returned to China. It completely violates Japan's commitment to China", and that there was "no way" for China to accept that.[^cite_note-280] In the days following the [Japanese general election](77996830.md) on 8 February 2026, [Spamouflage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spamouflage)-linked accounts targeted Sanae Takaichi.[^cite_note-281] On 8 March, Wang Yi stated "If something happens in China’s Taiwan region, what gives Japan the right to exercise the right to self-defence? Does the so-called collective self-defence right mean that Japan intends to hollow out its pacifist constitution, which renounces the right of belligerency?".[^cite_note-282]

On 30 March, China announced sanctions against [Keiji Furuya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keiji_Furuya).[^cite_note-283] On 21 April, after Japan relaxed the Three Principles on Transfer of Defence Equipment and Technology, which restricted its exports of arms abroad, Chinese Ministry of Foreign Affairs spokesperson Guo Jiakun expressed "serious concern" and stated the need of "heightened vigilance to resolutely resist Japan’s reckless moves towards new-style militarism".[^cite_note-284] On 29 April, Sun Xiaobo, director of the Arms Control Department of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, stated "Japan, as a defeated country in World War II, refuses to acknowledge the status. It is pushing to revise its pacifist constitution and Three Non-Nuclear Principles, expand its long-range strike capabilities and have nuclear weapons deployed on its territory by its ally".[^cite_note-\:4-285] On 30 April, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs released a document calling on the United Nations Security Council to make Japan's potential pursuit of nuclear arms an "important agenda item" and stating "Japan has long produced and stockpiled plutonium materials far exceeding the actual needs of its civilian nuclear energy programme, giving it the capability to achieve a ‘nuclear breakout’ within a short period" and adding "Allowing right-wing forces in Japan to push for the development of powerful offensive weapons, or even the possession of nuclear weapons, would inevitably once again bring harm to the international community and have seriously negative impacts on international peace, security and stability".[^cite_note-\:4-285] On 6 May, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs Lin Jian condemned Japan's "offensive missile" test at the Balikatan 2026 joint drill held by the Philippines and the United States, stating that "The former aggressor has not only failed to deeply reflect on its historical crimes but has instead dispatched military forces overseas and launched offensive missiles under the guise of so-called ‘security cooperation’".[^cite_note-286] In August 2026, Chinese Ambassador to Russia [Zhang Hanhui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhang_Hanhui) published an article in [TASS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TASS) where raised concerns over Japan’s large plutonium stockpile, saying it could be used to develop nuclear weapons if Japan changed its policy. Japan rejected the concerns and reiterated its Three Non-Nuclear Principles and its commitment to using nuclear energy for peaceful purposes.[^cite_note-287]

#### State media

Chinese state media criticized Takaichi's remarks intensively. Starting on 12 November, the [China Media Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Media_Group)'s [Yuyuan Tantian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuyuan_Tantian) published two commentaries on its Chinese social media account where it criticized Sanae Takaichi's remarks with insults.[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289] On the same day, [Hu Xijin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hu_Xijin), former editor-in-chief of the CCP-owned tabloid *[Global Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Times)*, called Takaichi, on X, an evil witch who had ignited a new round of mutual hatred between the Chinese and Japanese people,[^cite_note-290] though he later said on 25 November that state media used harsh language that does not reflect the actual situation when criticizing Takaichi and Japan and warned against "exaggerated" and "superficial" information.[^cite_note-291] On 14 November, the *[People's Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Daily)* published an editorial under the pseudonym "Zhong Sheng", used to represent the CCP's official view on foreign policy issues, which called Takaichi's statement "utterly isolating political nonsense" and said its aim was "breaking through the restraints of the peaceful constitution" and "seeking a pretext for Japanese military expansion".[^cite_note-Dotson-257] On 15 November, *Beijing Daily* compared Takaichi's comments to those which the [Japanese Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan) gave to justify its [invasion of Manchuria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_invasion_of_Manchuria).[^cite_note-Nurmamat-292]

On 16 November, the *[People's Liberation Army Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Liberation_Army_Daily)* published an article which said that if Japan intervened in a war between China and Taiwan, then its government would be "tying its nation to a chariot of self-destruction".[^cite_note-Nurmamat-292] Through an account that it operated on Chinese social media, the daily had on 13 November released posters in both Chinese and Japanese which compared Japan's intervention in Taiwan Strait affairs to Japan's invasion of China in World War II.[^cite_note-293] On 17 November, the *People's Daily* published another editorial under "Zhong Sheng", warning that "Japan risks universal condemnation if it insists on tying itself to the cart of breaking up China, \[and] it will inevitably suffer the results of its own evil actions".[^cite_note-Dotson-257] On 18 November, [Xinhua News Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xinhua_News_Agency) published an article saying Takaichi's statements were summoning militarist demons.[^cite_note-294] The *Global Times* published an editorial on 19 November which questioned Japanese sovereignty over [Okinawa](22477.md).[^cite_note-295] On 22 November, the *People's Daily* published an editorial which accused Takaichi of propagating the '[China threat theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_threat_theory)' and described her words and actions as warmongering that would eventually lead Japan to its demise.[^cite_note-296]

On 12 December, *PLA Daily* published an article by Xu Yizhen, which said Takaichi had "openly turned back the wheel of history" and that "any appeasement or tolerance of these provocative moves by Japanese right-wing politicians will only allow the ghost of militarism to rise again and once more place the peoples of Asia in peril".[^cite_note-297] On 15 December, a *People's Daily* "Zhong Sheng" editorial said Takaichi was "launching a serious provocation" on Taiwan, saying "Japan has never truly reflected on its historical crimes, and right-wing forces have never ceased attempting to rewrite history. Today, there is even a risk of militarism rising from the ashes".[^cite_note-Wang-267] On 18 December, the *PLA Daily* published two articles criticizing Japan's "militarization of space".[^cite_note-298] On 22 December, it published an article attacking Japan for having "no respect for history", saying that "instead, there is only a malicious attempt to cover up war crimes by using selective stories and wordplay, aiming to completely rewrite the facts of the invasion as generations pass".[^cite_note-299] On 25 December, the *People's Daily* published an editorial which said that "Post-war Japan has failed to carry out a thorough reckoning with militarism... This ‘[neo-militarism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-militarism)’, cloaked in the guise of a ‘peaceful nation’, is gradually becoming a real danger to Asia and the world at large".[^cite_note-300]

On 2 January 2026, the *[Study Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Study_Times)* published an editorial stating Japanese militarism was infiltrating culture and sports.[^cite_note-301] On 8 January, the China Arms Control and Disarmament Association and the China Institute of Nuclear Industry Strategy jointly released a 29-page report titled *Nuclear Ambitions of Japan’s Right-Wing Forces: A Serious Threat to World Peace*, which said recent actions by Japanese "right wing forces" were causing a "serious threat" to world peace and alleged Japan was seeking nuclear weapons.[^cite_note-Bloomberg_News-275] The report also claimed that statements from Japanese officials on acquisition of nuclear weapons were "not isolated or personal views".[^cite_note-japan-nukes-st-302] On 6 January, after China imposed trade restrictions on Japan, [Yuyuan Tantian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuyuan_Tantian), a social media account operated by China Central Television, characterized Japan's defence procurement system—in which the Ministry of Defense contracts production to private-sector firms—as a deliberate attempt to obscure military activities within its civilian industrial base, and stated China was "now moving to cut off this support at its root", adding that China's focus has moved beyond any single statement by Japanese government to the "systemic and fundamental issues" in the country.[^cite_note-Sun-276] On 24 March, Hua Dan and Zhang Li of the PLA Army Engineering University wrote an article at the *PLA Daily*, stating Japan's deployment of Type-12 missiles means it "is transitioning from a primarily defensive weapon to a long-range system capable of striking targets in neighbouring countries".[^cite_note-303] On the same day, an editorial by Zhong Sheng at the *People's Daily* criticizing Takaichi's economic policies, stating "The Takaichi administration’s obsession with militaristic adventurism is by no means a cure for the Japanese economy, but a poison that will accelerate its decay", stating Japan was "under the dual squeeze" of an "oil premium" and weakening [yen](34392.md), and accused of Takaichi of avoiding structural reforms in favor of defense, which it said "essentially ties Japan’s economy ever more tightly to the chariot of ‘new militarism’, with the ultimate cost borne by the Japanese people".[^cite_note-304]

### Japan

#### Government

On 10 November, Japanese chief cabinet secretary [Minoru Kihara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minoru_Kihara),[^cite_note-305] foreign minister [Toshimitsu Motegi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toshimitsu_Motegi),[^cite_note-306] and [Takayuki Kobayashi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takayuki_Kobayashi), chairman of the Policy Research Council of the [Liberal Democratic Party](164231.md) (LDP), all stated that Xue Jian's remarks were inappropriate.[^cite_note-307] On 11 November, the LDP held a joint meeting of its Foreign Affairs Committee and the Foreign Affairs Research Committee, where the participants agreed to require the government to expel Xue Jian as a *[persona non grata](521241.md).*[^cite_note-RFI-308] On 13 November, former prime minister [Shigeru Ishiba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shigeru_Ishiba) said that Takaichi's remarks were not conducive to enhancing Japan's deterrence capabilities and that successive Japanese cabinets avoided directly responding to questions regarding situations involving Taiwan.[^cite_note-309] On 15 November, in response to China's travel advisory against Japan, Kihara asked China to take appropriate actions and said communication between the two countries was essential.[^cite_note-310] On the same day, the three parties in the [Osaka City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osaka_City_Council), namely the LDP, [Komeito](163859.md), and [Osaka Restoration Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osaka_Restoration_Association), proposed a resolution demanding that Xue Jian apologize, which passed without objection.[^cite_note-311]

On 16 November, LDP acting policy research committee chairman [Norihisa Tamura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norihisa_Tamura) stated Takaichi had not provided sufficient background information during her remarks and that she should not make such statements in the future. Tamura also criticized Xue Jian's remarks, believing that they encouraged terrorism.[^cite_note-q571-312] On 18 November, in response to questions about China's travel advisory against Japan, [Kimi Onoda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimi_Onoda), Japan's economic security minister, said the country needed to see the dangers of economic dependence on China given its willingness to use economic coercion in order to settle disputes.[^cite_note-313] On the same day, [Hirofumi Yoshimura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hirofumi_Yoshimura), leader of the [Japan Innovation Party](48459506.md), condemned Xue Jian's remarks, saying that "a red line has been crossed".[^cite_note-RFI-308] He also said that preparations should be made to reduce the economic risks brought about by the decrease in Chinese tourists.[^cite_note-314] On 19 November, *[The Asahi Shimbun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Asahi_Shimbun)* reported that Takaichi privately told people around her that she "went a bit too far" and "gave an answer without fully checking what the fallout would be".[^cite_note-315]

The [Japan External Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_External_Trade_Organization) (JETRO) stated that as of 20 November, more than 20 Sino-Japanese business-related events had been cancelled.[^cite_note-316] On 21 November, when asked whether she would retract her comments, Takaichi refused, saying Japan's stance remained consistent and that her government would decide whether an event reached a survival-threatening threshold when the time came.[^cite_note-317] On 22 November, Transport Minister [Yasushi Kaneko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasushi_Kaneko) said the decline in tourism numbers from China was not something to worry about given the increase in arrivals from other countries.[^cite_note-318] On 25 November, in response to a question about Trump's public silence on Japan's dispute with China, Motegi said it has never been the case that the White House or the State Department has commented on every issue that could affect it.[^cite_note-319] On 26 November, Takaichi told the parliament regarding her comments that she "did not want to go into specifics", but "simply repeated the government’s previous answers over and over, there was a risk that the Budget Committee could be stalled". She also said Japan was "not in a position to recognize Taiwan's legal status".[^cite_note-320] On 1 December, a cross-party delegation from the [Japan–China Friendship Parliamentarians' Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan%E2%80%93China_Friendship_Parliamentarians'_Union) led by secretary-general [Yūko Obuchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Y%C5%ABko_Obuchi) of the LDP met with Chinese ambassador Wu Jianghao. The group, which included both LDP and opposition party members, expressed their hope to visit China later this year.[^cite_note-321]

On 3 December, Takaichi stated to the Japanese parliament that the "Japanese government's basic position regarding Taiwan remains as stated in the 1972 [Japan–China Joint Communiqué](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan%E2%80%93China_Joint_Communiqu%C3%A9), and there has been no change to this position". The position is that "the government of the People’s Republic of China reiterates that Taiwan is an inalienable part of the territory of the People’s Republic of China" and that Japan "fully understands and respects this stand" and "firmly maintains its stand under Article 8 of the [Potsdam Declaration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potsdam_Declaration)".[^cite_note-322] China refused Takaichi's explanation, with Chinese foreign ministry spokesperson Lin Jian stating Takaichi is "still prevaricating by claiming that the Japanese side’s position remains unchanged" and demanded a full retraction of Takaichi's 7 November statement.[^cite_note-323] In the same day, LDP vice president [Tarō Asō](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tar%C5%8D_As%C5%8D) said Takaichi's 7 November statement was "not problematic", saying that she "simply stated her consistent stance in more detail".[^cite_note-d0412-324] Several news agencies reported that Japan had expressed its concerns to France about China's possible participation in the [2026 G7 summit](77292926.md), where French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) was reportedly considering inviting Chinese leader Xi Jinping.[^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326] On 13 December, after Chinese foreign minister Wang Yi talked with France, Germany and the United Kingdom regarding Japan, [National Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Council_\(Japan\)) director-general Keiichi Ichikawa contacted the three countries to state that Japan's position on Taiwan had not changed.[^cite_note-327]

On 15 December, after questioning by [Japanese Communist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Communist_Party) (JCP) lawmaker [Taku Yamazoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taku_Yamazoe), Japanese foreign minister Toshimitsu Motegi told the Japanese parliament that "Regarding Taiwan, Japan's fundamental policy is, as the prime minister has clearly stated, in line with the 1972 Japan–China Joint Communique", saying that the "document states that the Government of Japan fully understands and respects this stand of the Government of the People’s Republic of China, and it firmly maintains its stand under Article 8 of the [Potsdam Proclamation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potsdam_Declaration)", referencing the PRC's position that Taiwan is a part of China.[^cite_note-Wang-2-328] After further questioning by Yamazoe, Motegi said that "Article 8 of the Potsdam Declaration incorporates the provisions of the [Cairo Declaration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1943_Cairo_Declaration)", and said that while Japan was "not a party to the Cairo Declaration itself", the Potsdam Declaration "stipulates that its provisions must be carried out". and stated "the Cairo Declaration set out the policy objectives of the [Allied Powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_II) at the time, including the return of [Manchuria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchuria), Taiwan and other territories from Japan to what was then the [Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_\(1912%E2%80%931949\)). \[The Potsdam Declaration] states that the terms shall be carried out", and said "Japan accepted the Potsdam Declaration".[^cite_note-Wang-2-328] The Chinese foreign ministry spokesperson Guo Jiakun noted the statement, but also said that "the Japanese side did not reiterate the Japanese government’s recognition that the government of the People’s Republic of China is the sole legal government of China, or that Taiwan is an inalienable part of the territory of the People’s Republic of China" and "deliberately sidestepped the important information that they are 'territories Japan has stolen from the Chinese'".[^cite_note-329]

On 17 December, Takaichi said in a press conference after an extraordinary Diet session that China is an "important neighbor" and that Japan wants to "continue frank dialogue and comprehensively promote a mutually beneficial relationship \[with China] based on common strategic interests".[^cite_note-330][^cite_note-331] On 25 December, Takaichi said that "As neighboring countries, there do tend to be areas of concern and challenges, but that is precisely why communication on all levels, including at the leadership level, is so important", adding that Japan is "open to all sorts of dialogue with China. We have not closed that door".[^cite_note-Murakami-2-209] On 18 December, an unnamed official in the prime minister's office suggested that Japan should consider acquiring nuclear weapons.[^cite_note-332] This resulted in a strong reaction from China's foreign ministry, who responded that it was a "serious issue that exposes the dangerous attempts by some in Japan to breach international law and possess nuclear weapons" and regarded that as a "signal of the resurgence of Japanese militarism".[^cite_note-japan-nukes-china-scmp-333][^cite_note-japan-nukes-st-302] The Japanese government later clarified that Japan's [non-nuclear weapons policy has not changed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan's_non-nuclear_weapons_policy), but did not comment on the official's subsequent status.[^cite_note-334] Conservatives in Japan has expressed similar support for nuclear weapons, including the *[Sankei Shimbun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sankei_Shimbun)* in its editorial.[^cite_note-japan-nukes-china-scmp-333]

On 27 January 2026, in response to a questioning by an opposition party leader, Takaichi told a TV program that "I want to make it absolutely clear that this is not about Japan going out and taking military action if China and the United States come into conflict (over Taiwan)", continuing that "If something serious happens there, we would have to go to rescue the Japanese and American citizens in Taiwan. In that situation, there may be cases where we take joint action. And if the U.S. forces, acting jointly with us, come under attack and Japan does nothing and simply runs away, the Japan–U.S. alliance would collapse. So we would respond strictly within the limits of the law — within the limits of the laws as they currently exist — while making a comprehensive judgment based on what is happening on the ground".[^cite_note-335] In March 2026, Japan dropped the phrase "one of Japan's most important bilateral relations" to describe relations with China in its draft [Diplomatic Bluebook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diplomatic_Bluebook_of_Japan), instead describing China as an "important neighbor".[^cite_note-336] On 17 June, Japanese defense minister Shinjirō Koizumi questioned the accuracy and transparency of China's [official military budget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_budget_of_China) figures, while stating Japan's budget "undergo scrutiny and deliberation in parliament";[^cite_note-337] the Chinese Foreign Ministry said Koizumi's claims were "inconsistent with facts" and "riddled with flaws", designed to mislead the international community.[^cite_note-338]

#### Opposition parties

On 11 November, the [Constitutional Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Democratic_Party_of_Japan) (CDP), Komeito and the [Democratic Party For the People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_For_the_People) (DPFP) issued protests against Xue Jian's comments.[^cite_note-RFI-308] CDP Secretary-General [Jun Anzumi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jun_Azumi) criticized them as not beneficial to bilateral relations while DPFP leader [Yuichiro Tamaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuichiro_Tamaki) said they had crossed the bottom line and hinted that Xue might be asked to leave Japan.[^cite_note-RFI-308] On the same day, [Renhō](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renh%C5%8D), a CDP councillor who was born a citizen of Taiwan, posted on X that Xue's comments damaged trust between Japan and China.[^cite_note-339] On 13 November, Komeito leader [Tetsuo Saito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tetsuo_Saito) questioned Takaichi's position on a Taiwan emergency and said he would submit questions to the government to clarify its stance on the matter.[^cite_note-340] On 11 November, Saito had expressed concern to the [Chinese Embassy in Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_China,_Tokyo) that Xue's actions were inconsistent with his status as a diplomat.[^cite_note-RFI-308] On 15 November, Japanese Communist Party (JCP) policy committee member Taku Yamazoe called on Takaichi to retract her comments.[^cite_note-341] On the same day, former prime minister and CDP leader [Yoshihiko Noda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoshihiko_Noda) said that Takaichi's remarks had a significant impact and needed to be taken seriously.[^cite_note-342]

On 16 November, Tomofumi Honjo, the policy research chairman of the CDP, said that Sanae Takaichi did not have a sufficient understanding of the relevant security legislation or of China's stance on the Taiwan issue.[^cite_note-q571-312] He also said that he hoped Japan would proceed with calm dialogue.[^cite_note-jiji-343] On 19 November, [Motohisa Furukawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motohisa_Furukawa), acting representative of the DPFP, said that the Japanese government needed to ask China to explain its response and, if necessary, ask it to withdraw Xue's comments.[^cite_note-jiji-343] On the same day, [Social Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Democratic_Party_\(Japan\)) leader [Mizuho Fukushima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mizuho_Fukushima), called on Takaichi to retract her remarks, arguing they were causing economic damage to Japanese society.[^cite_note-jiji-343] [Conservative Party of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_of_Japan) leader [Naoki Hyakuta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naoki_Hyakuta) said that the statement was justified and that it does not have to be retracted. He also congratulated the fall in the number of Chinese tourists.[^cite_note-344] On 23 November JCP Chair [Tomoko Tamura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomoko_Tamura) said in an interview that Takaichi should retract her remarks on Taiwan, as otherwise relations with China would deteriorate further.[^cite_note-345] On 26 November, CDP leader Yoshihiko Noda argued that Takaichi not mentioning any specific examples regarding Taiwan during the parliament meeting amounted to a "de facto retraction", an explanation which Chinese foreign ministry spokesperson Guo Jiakun refused by saying "Ceasing to mention the remarks and retracting them are two entirely different matters".[^cite_note-346] JCP Central Committee chairman [Kazuo Shii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazuo_Shii) also said he did not believe that Takaichi had retracted her statement.[^cite_note-d0412-324]

On 10 December, Komeito leader Tetsuo Saito said that he had held one in-person meeting and multiple phone calls with the Chinese ambassador Wu Jianghao since Takaichi's remarks, adding that he hoped to visit China in 2026 and would be willing to take a letter on Takaichi's behalf if requested.[^cite_note-347] On 12 December, CDP lawmaker [Kiyomi Tsujimoto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiyomi_Tsujimoto) said she obtained a script from the [Cabinet Secretariat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_Secretariat_\(Japan\)) made for Takaichi in advance of her testimony to the Diet in November, which showed the planned response for any questions regarding Taiwan was for Takaichi to decline to comment on hypothetical situations such as a Taiwan crisis.[^cite_note-348]

#### News media

On 10 November, *Yomiuri Shimbun*'s English-language newspaper *The Japan News* said that Xue's comment was inappropriate and that it was natural for the Japanese government to lodge a protest against it.[^cite_note-349] Follow up editorials which it published on 18 and 20 November criticized China's pressure campaign against Japan and called on Japan to continue its protests against China's actions and to make efforts to explain itself.[^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351] On 14 November, *[The Japan Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Japan_Times)* said Xue's comments were both offensive and in bad taste and a follow up editorial on 21 November called on Japan not to bend to China's campaign of economic coercion.[^cite_note-352][^cite_note-353]

On 11 November, *[The Mainichi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Mainichi)* said Takaichi's comments left the door open to dangerous over-interpretation and revealed a lack of awareness of the serious way that the Taiwan contingency scenario had impacted Japan-China relations.[^cite_note-354] An initial follow up editorial on 18 November called for de escalation from both Japan and China while a second follow up on 21 November called for Japan to continue engaging in dialogue with China in spite of the countermeasures it had imposed on Japan which the publication described as anti-Japan in nature.[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356] On the same day, *[Chunichi Shimbun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chunichi_Shimbun)* criticized her remarks as reckless and careless and said she should be more careful with her words in the future.[^cite_note-357] An initial follow up editorial on 18 November called for the two countries to continue communicating while a second follow up on 24 November described Takaichi's remarks as a provocation to China and said the opposition parties must not hesitate to criticize her government's policies and actions.[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359]

On 15 November, *The Nikkei* said that although China's response to Takaichi's remarks indicated it had lost its composure, the two countries nevertheless needed to talk in order to prevent conflict escalation.[^cite_note-360] A follow up editorial on 25 November said Japan-U.S. solidarity was essential to countering China's propaganda offensive against Japan.[^cite_note-361] On 18 November, *The Asahi Shimbun* said Takaichi needlessly heightened tensions with her choice of words while also expressing doubts about the logic of China's response.[^cite_note-362]

#### Public opinion

According to a poll published by Kyodo News after Takaichi's remarks, 48.8% of respondents supported activating collective self-defense in the event of a conflict over Taiwan, while 44.2% opposed it. The poll also showed that the latest approval rating of the [Takaichi Cabinet](81255380.md) was 69.9%, an increase of 5.5 percentage points from October.[^cite_note-363] According to a Nikkei/[TV Tokyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV_Tokyo) survey conducted from 28 to 30 November 55% of respondents believed Takaichi's comments were appropriate and 30% called it inappropriate. The survey also showed the approval rating of the Takaichi Cabinet was 75%.[^cite_note-364] According to a [Japan News Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_News_Network) opinion poll in December, 27% of respondents said they believed Takaichi's statement was a problem, while 55% said they did not. Additionally, 51% said they were anxious about the future of relations between China and Japan, while 46% said they were not.[^cite_note-365] According to another poll by [All-Nippon News Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-Nippon_News_Network) in December, 66% of Japanese respondents said they were concerned about the deterioration of relations with China.[^cite_note-Murakami-135]

According to a government survey in January 2026, 68% of Japanese respondents picked advances in Chinese military technology and its activities close to Japan and in the South China Sea as their top security worry, taking over worries from North Korea's missile program.[^cite_note-366] According to an NHK poll in the same month, 67% of Japanese respondents were concerned about the impact of export controls announced by China on Japan.[^cite_note-367] Takaichi's stance on China, including her statements that sparked this diplomatic crisis, was cited as a factor of LDP's landslide win in the 2026 Japanese general election resulting in LDP gaining a supermajority in the Lower House.[^cite_note-368][^cite_note-369] According to a poll by *The Asahi Shimbun* in September 2026, 57% of Japanese responders thought that Takaichi should make efforts to improve ties with China, including 58% of LDP members.[^cite_note-\:6-75]

#### Chinese embassy attack

Main article: [Chinese Embassy in Tokyo attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Embassy_in_Tokyo_attack)

On 24 March 2026, a person who claimed to be an active officer of [Japan Self-Defence Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_Self-Defence_Forces) forcibly entered the [Chinese embassy in Tokyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_China,_Tokyo), who threatened to "kill Chinese diplomatic personnel in the name of god". The spokesman for the Chinese foreign ministry [Lin Jian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lin_Jian) stated that "China is deeply shocked by this incident and has lodged a strong protest with Japan".[^cite_note-370][^cite_note-371] The suspect was later arrested and identified as Kodai Murata **(村田晃大)**, a member of the [Japan Ground Self-Defense Force](2978818.md) from [Ebino Garrison](https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%81%88%E3%81%B3%E3%81%AE%E9%A7%90%E5%B1%AF%E5%9C%B0) in Miyazaki Prefecture.[^cite_note-\:0-372][^cite_note-\:10-373] He was formally arrested for having a knife with a length of 18 centimeters when he broke into the embassy.[^cite_note-\:0-372]

## International reactions

### Taiwan

On 10 November, Presidential Office spokesperson Karen Kuo said Xue's remarks exceeded diplomatic etiquette and that the government took them seriously.[^cite_note-374] On 11 November, Taiwan's [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Taiwan\)) said that "Republic of China (Taiwan) is a sovereign, independent nation" and said it was not subordinate to the PRC.[^cite_note-Dotson-257] On 14 November, [Mainland Affairs Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainland_Affairs_Council) deputy minister Shen Yu-chung said China was ignoring international appeals for peace in the Taiwan Strait and was instead threatening countries that expressed pro-Taiwan sentiments.[^cite_note-375] On 15 November, [National Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Council_\(Taiwan\)) secretary-general [Joseph Wu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Wu) said China chose to continue using humiliating rhetoric against Japan instead of reflecting on the insulting nature of Xue's remarks.[^cite_note-Su-88] On 16 November, former president [Ma Ying-jeou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma_Ying-jeou), a member of the [Kuomintang](16903.md), accused Takaichi of "reckless adventurism", saying [cross-strait relations](2596399.md) were an "internal matter" that Japan should not intervene in.[^cite_note-376] Former Kuomintang chairwoman [Hung Hsiu-chu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hung_Hsiu-chu) also criticized Takaichi's remarks, saying that Taiwan was "no longer Japan’s colony" and that Takaichi was "overreaching" and "reckless".[^cite_note-Chung-377] In response, [Democratic Progressive Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Progressive_Party) (DPP) legislator [Rosalia Wu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosalia_Wu) criticized the Kuomintang for "failing to distinguish between friend and foe" while [Chen Kuan-ting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chen_Kuan-ting), also a DPP legislator, accused Ma of echoing Beijing's view of the crisis.[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379]

On 17 November, Taiwanese president [Lai Ching-te](22609914.md) described China's measures against Japan as a hybrid attack that undermined regional peace and stability and called on the country to exercise restraint.[^cite_note-380] Taiwanese foreign minister [Lin Chia-lung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lin_Chia-lung) said Takaichi's statements were consistent with those made by Shinzo Abe that had linked Taiwan's security directly to Japan's.[^cite_note-Chung-377] On the same day, Kuomintang chairwoman [Cheng Li-wun](8714312.md) accused Lai of inflaming tensions and said leaders should instead show caution and restraint.[^cite_note-Chung-377] On 18 November, in response to a question about changes in relations between Japan and China stemming from Xue's comments, [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Taiwan\)) spokesperson Hsiao Kuang-wei called on China to stop taking provocative actions and that Taiwan would continue to work with like-minded countries to uphold regional stability and security.[^cite_note-381] On 20 November, Lai published pictures of himself eating Japanese-sourced [sushi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sushi) in a show of support to Japan.[^cite_note-382] On the same day, Foreign Minister Lin said more people from Taiwan should travel to Japan and buy its products as a show of support for the country.[^cite_note-383] On 21 November, the [Ministry of Health and Welfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_and_Welfare_\(Taiwan\)) announced that Taiwan would lift all import control measures on Japanese food products.[^cite_note-384] Several DPP politicians including [Wang Yi-chuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Yi-chuan) visited Japan as a show of support.[^cite_note-385]

On 23 November, an assessment produced by the [National Security Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Bureau_\(Taiwan\)) said the crisis would accelerate Japan's efforts at implementing security reforms.[^cite_note-386] On 25 November, Taiwanese premier [Cho Jung-tai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cho_Jung-tai) said that "reunification" with China was "not an option".[^cite_note-Dotson-257] On 27 November, several members of the [New Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Party_\(Taiwan\)) protested in front of the [Japan–Taiwan Exchange Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan%E2%80%93Taiwan_Exchange_Association).[^cite_note-387] On 5 December, during a meeting with Japan's representative in Taiwan Shuzo Sumi, Cho said that Takaichi's statements "moved us all very, very much. They represent justice and peace" and welcomed Japanese performance groups and pop idols to Taiwan.[^cite_note-388] On 22 December, LDP executive acting secretary-general [Kōichi Hagiuda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C5%8Dichi_Hagiuda) led a group of LDP politicians to visit Taiwan; the delegation met with President Lai.[^cite_note-Reuters-271] In addition, three LDP lawmakers – [Keisuke Suzuki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keisuke_Suzuki), [Akihisa Nagashima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akihisa_Nagashima) and [Junichi Kanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junichi_Kanda) – as well as a five-person delegation led by [Hirofumi Takinami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hirofumi_Takinami) visited Taiwan around the same period.[^cite_note-Zhuang-272]

### United States

On 12 November, the [United States State Department](31975.md) issued a statement to Nikkei Asia which said the country was committed to maintaining peace and stability across the Taiwan Strait and opposed any unilateral changes to the status quo by either side.[^cite_note-389] When asked during a 10 November [Fox News](11121.md) interview whether China could not be considered a friend of the United States due to Xue's threatening comments about Takaichi, US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) did not offer support for Takaichi,[^cite_note-Sevastopulo-390] and said that "Well, a lot of our allies aren’t our friends, either. Our allies took advantage of us on trade more than China did".[^cite_note-391][^cite_note-Chung-377][^cite_note-392]

Bloomberg News noted that Trump and high-ranking US government officials did not offer public support for Takaichi, while lower-level officials including US ambassador to Japan [George Edward Glass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Edward_Glass) spoke out on her behalf.[^cite_note-393] The Global Taiwan Institute also noted that the US government has largely refrained from any public involvement.[^cite_note-Dotson-257] *Financial Times* noted the US had given little public support other than statements made by Glass.[^cite_note-Sevastopulo-390] On 10 November, Glass said in a post on X that Xue's comment threatened Takaichi and the Japanese people.[^cite_note-Chen-11] From 15 to 16 November, Glass posted two messages on X: the first one thanked China for helping Japan and the United States deepen their ties[^cite_note-394] while the second, which contained a photo of Sanae Takaichi and Trump on the same stage at the *[USS George Washington](198834.md)*, said Japan and the United States remained committed to maintaining peace and stability across the Taiwan Strait.[^cite_note-395] On 20 November, after a meeting with Foreign Minister Toshimitsu Motegi, Glass called China's ban on Japanese seafood a case of economic coercion and reaffirmed the country's obligation to defend Japan, which he said included the Senkaku islands.[^cite_note-396] On the same day, the State Department spokesperson Tommy Pigott said America's treaty commitment to Japan's security was unwavering and that it opposed any unilateral attempts to change the regional status quo;[^cite_note-397] the *Financial Times* reported that the US officials had promised to give a strong statement of support for Japan but that Japanese officials were "disappointed" to learn that statement was a post by Pigott.[^cite_note-Sevastopulo-390]

On 24 November, President Trump held back to back calls with Chinese leader Xi Jinping and Japanese prime minister Takaichi.[^cite_note-bn112425-398][^cite_note-nn112525-399] Both China and the United States said Trump initiated the calls.[^cite_note-nn112525-399] In a post on [Truth Social](69077351.md), Trump said he had a very good telephone call with Xi Jinping and that China–US relations were "extremely strong", adding they had discussed many topics, including the war in Ukraine, America's fentanyl crisis, and Chinese purchases of U.S. farm products;[^cite_note-bn112425-398][^cite_note-nn112525-399] the post did not mention Japan or Taiwan.[^cite_note-bn112425-398][^cite_note-nn112525-399] On 25 November, Trump said Xi had during their call more or less agreed to his request that China accelerate and increase purchases of American goods.[^cite_note-400] Trump subsequently held a call with Takaichi to brief her on his call with Xi.[^cite_note-401] According to *The Wall Street Journal* and Reuters, Trump advised Takaichi to not escalate tensions and not to provoke China on the question of Taiwan's sovereignty.[^cite_note-402][^cite_note-403] In reference to this reporting, Japanese chief cabinet secretary [Minoru Kihara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minoru_Kihara) said on 27 November that Trump had given no such advice.[^cite_note-404] In the same day, Kyodo News also reported Trump urged Takaichi to avoid escalating tensions with China.[^cite_note-405] On 14 December, *[Hokkaido Shimbun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hokkaido_Shimbun)* reported that Trump had told Takaichi that "the US and China are trying to get along, so don't get in the way" citing a government source, and that Takaichi was "quite depressed" after the meeting as Trump "didn't give Takaichi the answer she had in mind".[^cite_note-406]

On 7 December, *Financial Times* reported that according to Japanese officials, there was "deep disappointment" in the Japanese government over the lack of public support from US top officials, adding that Japanese ambassador to the US [Shigeo Yamada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shigeo_Yamada) had urged the Trump administration for public support.[^cite_note-Sevastopulo-390] On 11 December, White House spokesperson [Karoline Leavitt](71739774.md) said that Trump believes the US "should be in a position to have a good working relationship with China while maintaining our very strong alliance with Japan".[^cite_note-407] On 19 December, US Secretary of State [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) called tensions between China and Japan "preexisting", and said that he believes that "we feel very strongly that we can continue with our strong, firm partnership and alliance with Japan, and do so in a way that continues to allow us to find productive ways to work together with the [Chinese Communist Party](7175.md) and the [Chinese government](23239.md)".[^cite_note-408]

In March 2026, the [US Intelligence Community](501244.md) released the Annual Threat Assessment report, which devoted a large section covering Sino-Japanese relations. It wrote that Takaichi's "specific comments carry weight in Japan’s system because the phrase ‘survival threatening situation’ serves as a possible legal justification for military authorities" and that they "represent a significant shift for a sitting Japanese prime minister". In response, Chief Cabinet Secretary Minoru Kihara said that the "claim that there has been a significant shift in policy is inaccurate".[^cite_note-409] In May 2026, the *[Financial Times](136566.md)* reported that, at a meeting with Trump during [his visit](83136377.md) to China, Xi Jinping had condemned Prime Minister Takaichi for Japan's "remilitarisation", with Xi reportedly becoming "vocal and agitated when discussing Japan". Adding that this was the most intense part of Trump's visit, the *Financial Times* reported Trump had responded by saying Japan had to take a more assertive defense stance due to rising threats from North Korea.[^cite_note-410]

### Other countries

-  [Australia](4689264.md): On 7 December 2025, following Chinese jets locking their fire-control radar at Japanese jets, Australian defence minister [Richard Marles](4386805.md) stated that Australia was "deeply concerned" by China's actions and pledged to work with Japan in upholding a rules-based order,

  [^cite_note-Kelly-95]

  &#x20;though he did not address the spat over Takaichi's statements.

  [^cite_note-411]

-  [Cambodia](334751.md): On 27 November 2025, in reaction to the diplomatic crisis, the [Cambodian Ministry of Foreign Affairs and International Cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_and_International_Cooperation_\(Cambodia\)) released a statement saying "Cambodia strongly advocates for efforts to maintain regional peace, stability and harmony, based on the principle of mutual respect and peaceful resolution of differences and disputes". It said that Cambodia "resolutely adheres to the One-China Policy" and recognizes Taiwan as an "inalienable part of China’s territory", describing matters related to Taiwan as China's internal matters, and said Cambodia supports China's "efforts toward national reunification" and opposes Taiwan independence.

  [^cite_note-412]

-  [Laos](17752.md): On 28 November 2025, the [Lao Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Laos\)) stated that Laos firmly supports [UN General Assembly Resolution 2758](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_2758_\(XXVI\)), adheres to the one-China principle, recognizes that Taiwan is an inseparable part of the territory of the People's Republic of China, and opposes any interference in China's internal affairs.

  [^cite_note-413]

-  [Myanmar](19457.md): On 22 November 2025, Myanmar military junta's Deputy Minister of Information [Zaw Min Tun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaw_Min_Tun_\(general\)) told Xinhua News Agency Takaichi's remarks showed "no lessons learned from history" and "no remorse" for Japan's wartime crimes across Asia, including Myanmar. He said that "Myanmar firmly condemns any possible resurgence of fascist tendencies in Japan", accusing Japan of supporting Taiwan independence, and reaffirmed Myanmar's support for the [one China policy](66739.md).

  [^cite_note-Kavi-414]

  &#x20;*[The Irrawaddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Irrawaddy)* noted that since the junta seized power in [2021 in a coup d'état](66567997.md), it had relied heavily on China for support while Japan had downgraded its diplomatic ties it and instead maintained contacts with Myanmar's pro-democracy forces.

  [^cite_note-Kavi-414]

-  [North Korea](21255.md): On 12 November 2025, the [North Korean Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(North_Korea\)) issued a statement stating Japan has "kept provoking China", accusing the country of "using the Taiwan issue as a leverage for taking sides with the U.S. in the implementation of "Indo-Pacific Strategy", a tool for attaining its own politico-diplomatic goals", which it said would "only cause security concerns in the region and discord between nations".

  [^cite_note-415]

  &#x20;On 18 November, during an annual UN debate, North Korea said that the "international community still remembers Japan's unprecedented, heinous anti-humanity crimes in history".

  [^cite_note-416]

  &#x20;Starting from 11 January 2026, North Korea started to accuse Japan of "neo-militarism", adopting a similar wording from China.

  [^cite_note-417]

-  [Pakistan](23235.md): On 28 November 2025, in response to a question by the Xinhua News Agency regarding Takaichi's comments, [Pakistan Foreign Ministry](13048437.md) spokesperson Tahir Andrabi said that Pakistan was firmly supportive of the one China policy, unequivocally recognizing the People's Republic of China as the sole legitimate government representing all of China and Taiwan an inalienable part of China, and said Pakistan opposes any attempt to create [two Chinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two_Chinas), or [one China, one Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Country_on_Each_Side).

  [^cite_note-418]

-  [Russia](25391.md): On 18 November 2025, Foreign Ministry spokeswoman [Maria Zakharova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Zakharova) said in an interview with Xinhua News Agency in [Moscow](19004.md) that Takaichi's remarks were "extremely dangerous and that Japan should deeply reflect on its history and learn from the lessons of World War II to avoid serious consequences caused by wrong words and deeds".

  [^cite_note-419]

  &#x20;On 20 November, Zakharova said that "Not only does Japan fail to reflect on the mistakes of its recent remarks, but it also refuses to acknowledge the results of World War II even 80 years later".

  [^cite_note-420]

  &#x20;After a meeting between Chinese Foreign Minister [Wang Yi](18211536.md) and [Russian Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_Council_of_Russia) Secretary [Sergei Shoigu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergei_Shoigu) on 3 December, the two countries released a joint statement saying that they had "conducted strategic alignment on issues related to Japan, reaching a high degree of consensus" and "agreed to resolutely uphold the outcomes of World War II victory, firmly oppose any attempts to whitewash colonial aggression and resolutely counter any attempts to revive fascism or Japanese militarism". Shoigu added that "the hydra of militarism is once again raising its head" but that China and Russia "will not allow the revival of criminal regimes in Europe and Tokyo".

  [^cite_note-421]

  &#x20;On 28 December, Russian Foreign Minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md) gave an interview to [TASS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TASS), where he said Russia opposes [Taiwan independence](31156.md) and considers Taiwan to be an inseparable part of China, while urging Japan to "think carefully" before entering a "course towards militarization".

  [^cite_note-422]

-  [Singapore](27318.md): On 19 November 2025, Prime Minister [Lawrence Wong](32336575.md) stated that he hoped China and Japan would "find ways to resolve these very complex issues and move forward", saying that "[Southeast Asia](28741.md) has done that with Japan... We've put history aside and we’re moving forward". He declined to comment on Takaichi's remarks, but said a war over Taiwan was unlikely.

  [^cite_note-423]

-  [South Korea](27019.md): On 3 December 2025, President [Lee Jae Myung](50305655.md) said that South Korea would not take sides between China and Japan, which he said "would only escalate the conflict", and called on both countries to "seek ways to coexist". He also offered for South Korea to "play a role in mediating and coordination" between the two countries.

  [^cite_note-424]

  &#x20;On 5 December, South Korean ambassador to Japan Lee Hyuk said that if South Korea maintained good relations with both China and Japan, it could act as a mediator between the two countries.

  [^cite_note-425]

  &#x20;On 7 January, during a visit to China, President Lee stated that South Korea was "keeping an eye on the situation", adding that "If you intervene in a reasonable quarrel between adults, you will probably end up resented by both sides". He also called China's dual-export restrictions on Japan "complicated and deep-rooted", while saying he hoped the issue would be "resolved". Lee also said that for South Korea, "relations with Japan are just as important as relations with China".

  [^cite_note-426]

  &#x20;After China's dual-export restrictions, the [Ministry of Trade, Industry and Resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Trade,_Industry_and_Resources) announced South Korea would "preemptively review whether it is possible to replace imported Japanese items subject to China’s export curbs on dual-use goods in order to minimize negative impacts on supply chains".

  [^cite_note-427]

-  [United Kingdom](31717.md): On 27 November, the Secretary of State for the Indo-Pacific [Seema Malhotra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seema_Malhotra) stated that she was "not in a position to comment on other countries' diplomacy" but added that she "hopes that any disputes over the Taiwan Strait will be resolved through dialogue".

  [^cite_note-428]

-  [Vietnam](202354.md): On 27 November, [Vietnamese Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Vietnam\)) spokesperson Phạm Thu Hằng said that "Based on the "One China" policy stance, Vietnam believes that peace, stability, and cooperation in the Taiwan Strait are of significance to the region and the world".

  [^cite_note-429]
